Asymptotic Weak Gravity Conjecture in M-theory on K ​ 3 × K ​ 3 K3\times K3
M.Charkaoui, R. Sammani, E.H Saidi, R. Ahl Laamara

Abstract
The Asymptotic WGC has been proposed as a special case of the tower WGC that probes infinite distances in the moduli space corresponding to weakly coupled gauge regimes. The conjecture has been studied in M-theory on Calabi-Yau threefold (CY3) with finite volume inducing a 5D effective QFT. In this paper, we extend the scope of the previous study to encompass lower dimensions, particularly we generalise the obtained 5D asymptotic WGC to the effective field theory ( EFT 3D ) coupled to 3D gravity that descends from M-theory compactified on Calabi-Yau fourfold with an emphasis on K ​ 3 × K ​ 3 K3\times K3 . We find that the CY4 has three fibration structures labelled as line Type- 𝕋 2 \mathbb{T}^{2} , surface Type- 𝕊 \mathbb{S} and bulk Type- 𝕍 \mathbb{V} . The emergent EFT 3D is shown to have 2+2 towers of particles states termed as the BPS 𝒯 M k → 0 bps \mathcal{T}_{M_{\mathrm{k}}\rightarrow 0}^{\text{{bps}}} and 𝒯 M k → ∞ bps \mathcal{T}_{M_{\mathrm{k}}\rightarrow\infty}^{\text{{bps}}} as well as the non-BPS 𝒯 M k → 0 n-bps \mathcal{T}_{M_{\mathrm{k}}\rightarrow 0}^{\text{{n-bps}}} and 𝒯 M k → ∞ n-bps \mathcal{T}_{M_{\mathrm{k}}\rightarrow\infty}^{\text{{n-bps}}} . To ensure the viability of the 3D Asymptotic WGC, we give explicit calculations to thoroughly test the swampland constraint for both the weakly and strongly gauge coupled regimes. Additional aspects, including the gauge symmetry breaking and duality symmetry are also investigated.



1
Introduction

Indubitably, the Swampland Program represents one of the major leaps
and breakthroughs in our comprehension of quantum gravity
1
-
3C
. It provides a protocol to filter the effective field theories
admitting a UV completion based on a set of consistency constraints known as
Swampland Conjectures. Among the inaugural Swampland criteria, we cite (i)
the Weak Gravity Conjecture (WGC)
4A
-
8
expecting the
presence of at least one super-extremal particle. One of its most potent
refinements is (ii) the so-called Tower WGC predicting the existence of a
tower of massive charged states satisfying the WGC
9A
,
instead of the lone super-extremal particle required by the mild version;
and (iii) the Swampland Distance conjecture (SDC) stipulating the emergence
of an infinite tower of states that becomes asymptotically massless at large
distances in the moduli space
10
-
11B
.

Using principles of the tower WGC and SDC, a new version of the WGC was
obtained in
12
following an investigation of the
aforementioned conjectures in M- theory on Calabi-Yau threefold (CY3) with a
weak coupling regime
11A
;
12
; it is labeled the
Asymptotic-WGC. In fact, to study the resulting 5D effective field
theory (
\emph{EFT}_{5D}
) in the infinite distance limit, while gravity
remains dynamical, one would require that the volume
\mathcal{V}_{CY_{3}}

of the internal complex threefold stay finite (
\mathcal{V}_{CY_{3}}<\infty
). This condition on the volume imposes a certain
structure on the CY3 fibration:
\mathcal{F}_{fiber}\times\mathbb{B}_{base}
, to be
(1)
either of a line fiber of Type-
\mathbb{T}^{2}
; or
(2)
a
surface fiber of Type-K3 or Type-
\mathbb{T}^{4}
. For these three
Type-forms, the volume of the fiber
\mathcal{F}_{fiber}
shrinks to zero in
the infinite distance limit of the Kahler moduli space of the
EFT
5D
; while the volume of the base
\mathbb{B}_{base}
expands to infinity
in such a way that the full volume
\mathcal{V}_{CY_{3}}
of the CY3,
naively given by
\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{F}_{fiber}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\mathbb{B}_{base}},
remains finite.

The effective gauge theory coupled to gravity (which is now dynamical by
taking a finite CY volume) has (i) a weak gauge coupling regime in
the large distance limit (
g_{YM}\rightarrow 0
); and (ii) a charge lattice
\mathfrak{Q}
endowed by rays (charge directions) hosting towers of BPS and non-BPS states satisfying the WGC
12
. From this
perspective, the Asymptotic WGC can be seen as a special case of the Tower
WGC in the sense that it only involves a particular region of the moduli
space (i.e weakly coupled directions of the charge lattice); whereas the
Tower WGC expects towers of states in all directions independently of the
weak coupling limits. The weak gauge coupling regime is particularly
interesting because it supplies properties that are not allowed otherwise;
such as the relation between the extremality bound and the Repulsive Force
Conjecture
13
as well as the precise calculation of the
charge to mass ratio of non-BPS particle states
12
.

Furthermore, successful tests of the asymptotic WGC have been realized in
both F-theory on elliptically fibered Calabi-Yau fourfolds
14A
and M-theory on Calabi-Yau threefolds
12
. In the latter,
the infinite distance limit fixes the fibration structure to two Type-forms:
either Type-
\mathbb{T}^{2}
or Type-
K3
/
\mathbb{T}^{4}
. Therein, some
directions of the
EFT
5D
’s charge lattice hold the condition of
the weak gauge coupling regime formulated as
12
;
14A
:

\frac{\Lambda_{\text{{wgc}}}^{2}}{\Lambda_{\text{{qg}}}^{2}}\qquad\rightarrow\qquad 0

(1)

where
\Lambda_{\text{{wgc}}}
is the magnetic WGC scale of the

EFT
{}_{5D},
and
\Lambda_{\text{{qg}}}
is the species scale;
i.e the scale above which quantum gravity becomes strongly coupled
instigating therefore a change of its dynamics
14B
;
14C
. The above condition indicates the presence of weakly coupled
gauge groups along particular directions in the charge lattice of the
effective theory. In the context of
EFT
{}_{5D},
two types of towers
of states were shown to satisfy the conjecture, given by BPS and non-BPS
states. Other studies has focused solely on BPS states since the BPS bound
aligns with the super-extremality bound, as well as the relation between
their charge and mass fixed by supersymmetry
14D
-
14F
.

In this paper, we investigate the Asymptotic WGC at infinite distance limit
of the 3D effective field theory (
\emph{EFT}_{3D}
) coupled to 3D gravity.
This effective gauge theory descends from M-theory compactified on
Calabi-Yau fourfold (CY4=
X_{4}
) with finite volume (
\mathcal{V}_{CY_{4}}<\infty
) following the framework of the 5D EFT of
11A
and
12
. Note however that most studies in the
context of 3D gravity has focused on AdS theories
14G
;
6
particularly a study of the basic and tower WGC conjectures in 3D from the
perspective of infrared consistency was performed in
9A
;
9B
.

To probe the Asymptotic WGC in
EFT
3D
emerging from the
compactification of M-theory on a Calabi-Yau fourfold, we begin by outlining
the classification of the fibral structure of the CY4 in the infinite
distance limit. We find three Type-forms labeled as the line Type-
\mathbb{T}^{2}
, the surface Type-
\mathbb{S}
and the bulk Type-
\mathbb{V}
. The two
first Type-forms are common with the
CY3
fibral classification
11A
; infinite distance limits of CY3 has also been studied in
15
;
16
. The new bulk Type-
\mathbb{V}
is due to the higher
dimension of CY4 compared to CY3. This classification will allow us to keep
working in a regime where gravity is dynamical, by taking the volume
\mathcal{V}_{CY_{4}}
finite in the infinite distance limit, while the fiber

\mathcal{F}_{fiber}
of
X_{4}
shrinks to zero and its base
\mathbb{B}_{base}
expands to infinity.

Given the richness of the fibration structure of the Calabi-Yau
manifold, it would be more informative to focus on the surface Type-
\mathbb{S}
for the following reasons: First, the fibral surface
S_{\perp}
and
the base surface
S_{\parallel}
have the mid- dimension of
X_{4}
.
Second, this surface Type-
\mathbb{S}
permits the implementation of
discrete symmetries like the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
automorphism generated by the
transposition
S_{\perp}\longleftrightarrow

S_{\parallel}
leaving the
Calabi-Yau fourfold invariant. In particular in
12
only the
base was allowed to expand while the fiber shrinks for the purpose of providing a weak
gauge coupling regime; for our 3D investigation, we will take advantage of
the automorphism
S_{\perp}\longleftrightarrow

S_{\parallel}
to go
even further and allow both of them to expand or shrink as long as the total
volume remains finite. This property will generate the discrete symmetry
predicting the existence of two asymptotic limits of the WGC related by
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-symmetry: the usual asymptotic WGC with weak gauge coupling
investigated in 5D in addition to the asymptotic WGC derived in this paper
predicting a dual strong gauge coupling regime. Even though we are primarily
interested in the weak gauge coupling of the
EFT
3D
in the
infinite distance limit, we show that in the Type-
\mathbb{S}
fibration of
X_{4}
with
\mathbb{S}
taken as the K3 surface, both strong and weak
regimes arise as the gauge symmetry breaks into
\emph{weakly}
and
strongly
coupled gauge groups.

As for the towers of quantum states required by the conjecture, we
show that the particles of the
EFT
3D
generate four series given
by:
(\mathbf{i})
A tower
\mathcal{T}_{M_{\mathrm{k}}\rightarrow 0}^{\text{{bps}}}
of charged BPS particles with light masses that vanish in
the infinite distance limit (
M_{\mathrm{k}}

\rightarrow 0
); it is
labelled by the integer k.
(\mathbf{ii})
A tower
\mathcal{T}_{M_{\mathrm{k}}\rightarrow\infty}^{\text{{bps}}}
of charged BPS
particles with extremely heavy masses (
M_{\mathrm{k}}\rightarrow\infty
).

(\mathbf{iii})
A tower
\mathcal{T}_{M_{\mathrm{k}}\rightarrow 0}^{\text{{n-bps}}}
of non-BPS particles having light masses.
(\mathbf{iv})

A tower
\mathcal{T}_{M_{\mathrm{k}}\rightarrow\infty}^{\text{{n-bps}}}
of non BPS particles with extremely heavy masses. These
2+2
towers of
particle states are related amongst others by the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}

automorphism symmetry of the
\mathbb{S}_{\perp}\times

\mathbb{S}_{\parallel}
fibration and the Weak/Strong gauge symmetry duality of the
3D effective gauge theory. More precisely we use the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}

duality between weak and strong coupling limits to argue that in the
strongly coupled regions in the moduli space of the
EFT
3D
, we
can also expect the presence of super-extremal states, since these regions
can be seen as weakly coupled in a dual frame. In consequence, the
asymptotic WGC is supported by even more towers of states than previously
anticipated, which is in line with the tower WGC.

To sum up, these are the major results of this current investigation:

(1)

We classify the possible fibrations of the Calabi-Yau fourfold in
the infinite distance limit while maintaining a finite volume inducing an

EFT
3D
coupled to 3D gravity. We show that the
X_{4}
has three
different fibral structures labelled as Line Type-
\mathbb{T}^{2}
, Surface
Type-
\mathbb{S}
, and Bulk Type-
\mathbb{V}
. This classification
generalises the one obtained in
11A
for the
EFT
5D

coupled to 5D gravity induced by M-theory on CY3 in the infinite distance
limit.

(2)

Considering the Type-
\mathbb{S}
structure of the Calabi-Yau
fourfold namely
X_{4}

=

\mathbb{S}_{\perp}\times

\mathbb{S}_{\parallel}
with an emphasis on the case
K3_{\perp}\times K3_{\parallel}
, we generate a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
- automorphism symmetry by
allowing both the fiber and the base to either shrink or expand, provided
that the overall volume is finite. This discrete symmetry is given by the
transposition
K3_{\perp}\longleftrightarrow K3_{\parallel}
including the
permutation of their p-cycles. By taking advantage of the special properties
of K3, we work out the two asymptotic gauge regimes (weak and strong) of the

EFT
3D
that are compatible with the tower WGC. We also give two
claims (I and II) regarding the two gauge regimes of Asymptotic WGC that
exhibit the Weak/Strong duality of this 3D effective gauge theory.

(3)

Using the wrapping of M2 and M5 branes on K3 cycles as well as
the duality links with heterotic string
12
, we construct the
content of the four aforementioned towers namely: the content of the
\mathcal{T}_{M_{\mathrm{k}}\rightarrow 0}^{\text{{bps}}}
having
light BPS particles. The
\mathcal{T}_{M_{\mathrm{k}}\rightarrow\infty}^{\text{{bps}}}
having extremely heavy BPS particles. The content of
\mathcal{T}_{M_{\mathrm{k}}\rightarrow 0}^{\text{{n-bps}}}
with
light non-BPS particles. And the
\mathcal{T}_{M_{\mathrm{k}}\rightarrow\infty}^{\text{{n-bps}}}
made of extremely heavy non BPS particles.
These light and the heavy towers are related by the Weak/Strong gauge
symmetry duality.

(4)

Using the link between asymptotic WGC and the Repulsive Force
Conjecture (RFC)
13
, we give explicit verifications of the
Asymptotic WGC for both weakly gauge coupled and strongly gauge coupled
regimes in the
EFT
3D
. We perform this test first for the
towers
\mathcal{T}_{M_{\mathrm{k}}\rightarrow 0}^{\text{{bps}}}
and

\mathcal{T}_{M_{\mathrm{k}}\rightarrow\infty}^{\text{{bps}}}
made
of BPS particle states, then for
\mathcal{T}_{M_{\mathrm{k}}\rightarrow 0}^{\text{{n-bps}}}
and
\mathcal{T}_{M_{\mathrm{k}}\rightarrow\infty}^{\text{{n-bps}}}
made of non-BPS particle states. And because
classical massless 3D gravity is topological, we forfeit the classical
effects for a non trivial negative contribution
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}}_{QG}
originating from quantum corrections.

The organisation of this paper is as follows: In
section 2
, we
generalize the results of
11A
regarding
EFT
5D
,
induced by
X_{3}
in the infinite distance limit, towards
EFT
3D

descending from M-theory on Calabi-Yau fourfold
X_{4}
with finite volume.
In
section 3
, we investigate the physical implications of the
geometry probed by M-theory on CY4 in the moduli space of the 3D effective
theory. We give results on the gauge coupling regimes in the
EFT
3D
with
X_{4}=K3\times K3
. In
section 4
, we distinguish the
different gauge regimes before constructing the four towers of particle
states in
EFT
3D
candidates to satisfy the asymptotic WGC.
In
section 5
, we give explicit calculations testing the Asymptotic
WGC for both weak and strong regime. Last section is devoted to conclusions.
In
Appendix A
we report some technical details on the underlying
properties of the towers of BPS and non-BPS states.

2
Classification of fibers of CY4

In this section, we give first results on the large distance limit for
M-theory on Calabi-Yau fourfold (CY4) with finite volume (
\mathcal{V}_{{\small CY}_{4}}<\infty).
These results will be used later on for
compactified M-theory with background

\mathcal{M}_{11D}=X_{4}\times\mathcal{M}_{3D}\qquad;\qquad X_{4}:=CY_{4}

(2)

where the compact
X_{4}
is fibered like
\mathcal{F}_{n}\times\mathbb{B}_{4-n}
with complex fiber dimension taking the three values
n=1,2,3.

These results will be also used in the study of the Asymptotic Weak Gravity
Conjecture (Asymptotic-WGC) in 3D space-time. To do so, we need two main
properties.

(\mathbf{A})

being particularly interested in theories coupled to
3D (topological) gravity, we need to keep the volume of the complex 4d
internal manifold
X_{4}
finite

\mathcal{V}_{{\small CY}_{4}}:=\left(l_{11d}\right)^{8}\mathcal{V}_{{\small X}_{{\small 4}}}<\infty

(3)

where
l_{11d}=1/M_{11d}
is the usual 11D fundamental length scale of
M-theory
17A
. Notice that the
\mathcal{V}_{{\small X}_{{\small 4}}}
is dimensionless (
\left[\mathcal{V}_{{\small X}_{{\small 4}}}\right]=length^{0}
); and is related to the Planck mass
M_{\mathrm{Pl}}

in three dimensions and the 11D mass scale via
4{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\mathcal{V}_{{\small X}_{{\small 4}}}\simeq M_{Pl}/M_{11d};
i.e:

M_{11d}=\frac{M_{Pl}}{4{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\mathcal{V}_{{\small X}_{{\small 4}}}}

(4)

(\mathbf{B})

Below, we will be interested in the infinite distance
limit in the (gauge region of the) moduli space
\mathfrak{M}_{X_{4}}
of
the effective field theory (
EFT
3D
) descending from M-theory on
X_{4}
. In this limit, we will take the volume
\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{F}_{n}}
of the fiber to shrink to zero whereas the volume
\mathcal{V}_{\mathbb{B}_{4-n}}
of base diverges; i.e:

\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{F}_{n}}\rightarrow 0\qquad and\qquad\mathcal{V}_{\mathbb{B}_{4-n}}\rightarrow\infty

(5)

while their product
\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{F}_{n}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\mathbb{B}_{4-n}}
is finite.

Thus, we need to construct CY manifolds
X_{4}
which obey both the two
above properties:
\left(\mathbf{A}\right)
and
\left(\mathbf{B}\right).
And this is the main goal of the investigation given in this section. To
that purpose, we revisit in subsection
2.1
useful results on
EFT
5D
given by M-theory on Calabi-Yau threefolds CY
{}_{3}:=X_{3}

11A
;
12
. In subsection
2.2
, we give our results
on the fibration structure of the Calabi-Yau fourfold that induces
our
EFT
3D
in infinite distance limit in the moduli space.

As a front matter, notice that, as for finite distances in the moduli space
of
EFT
(11-2d)D
, one can also distinguish various possible fibrations
\mathcal{F}_{n}\times\mathbb{B}_{d-n}
of
complex d-dimensional
CY_{d}:=X_{d}
in the infinite distance limit (
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow\infty
). For the case
d=4
we are particularly
interested into here, we have the following typical fibrations

internal X
4

fiber
\mathcal{F}_{n}

base
\mathbb{B}_{4-n}

\left(a\right)

\mathcal{F}_{1}

\mathbb{B}_{3}

\left(b\right)

\mathcal{F}_{2}

\mathbb{B}_{2}

\left(c\right)

\mathcal{F}_{3}

\mathbb{B}_{1}

(6)

such that the first Chern class
c_{1}\left(\mathcal{F}_{n}\times\mathbb{B}_{4-n}\right)
vanishes. Note also that a fibered Calabi-Yau manifold must
have a fiber which is also Calabi-Yau
17B
, this will limit
tremendously the number of the possibilities, especially for
\mathcal{F}_{1}
and
\mathcal{F}_{2}.
On the other
hand the possibilities of
\mathcal{F}_{3}
are vastly
larger
18
;

To build the
\mathcal{F}_{n}
fibers and the corresponding
\mathbb{B}_{4-n}
bases that can be used in Asymptotic-WGC of
EFT

we borrow ideas from the classification of fiber structure of on

2.1
Classification of X
3
fibers in infinite distance limit

Following
11A
, we distinguish two kinds of fibrations in
M-theory on Calabi-Yau threefolds in the infinite distance limit with three
possible fibers (namely
\mathbb{T}^{2},
K3 and
\mathbb{T}^{4}
).
Depending on the dimension of the fiber, we have either

\mathcal{F}_{1}

\rightarrow

X
3

\downarrow

\mathbb{B}_{2}

(7)

with
\mathcal{F}_{1}

\mathcal{F}_{2}

\rightarrow

X

\downarrow

\mathbb{B}_{1}

(8)

and

in

\mathcal{V}_{{\small X}_{{\small 3}}}

We start by parameterizing the Kahler 2-form

with

\textstyle\mathchar 277

\left(\mathbf{1}\right)

Type-
\mathbb{T}^{2}

form:

It corresponds to
J_{0}^{3}=0
but

21
;

Here, the fiber

) is given by a

The Kahler form is given by

\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{F}_{1}}\sim\frac{1}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}}T_{finite}\qquad,\qquad\mathcal{V}_{\mathbb{B}_{2}}\sim{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}B_{finite}

and

. Notice that while
\lim_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow\infty}\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{F}_{1}}=0
and

, the total volume of the
X_{3}

remains finite even in the infinite distance limit where it takes the value

\lim_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow\infty}\mathcal{V}_{{\small CY}_{{\small 3}}}\simeq T_{finite}\times B_{finite}

(12)

\left(\mathbf{2}\right)

Type-
\mathbb{S}

forms:

It corresponds to

J_{0}^{3}=0
and
J_{0}^{2}=0

23A
. In this Type- surface form, the fiber

in the second

\left(\mathbf{b}\right)

having the topology of

.

For the generalization of this classification to the case of the

both of hem having

3
EFT
3D
descending from M-theory on
K3_{\bot}\times K3_{\|}

In this section, we study physical implications in the infinite distance
limit in the moduli space of the underlying 3D effective theory descending
from 11D M-theory on the background

\mathcal{M}_{11D}=X_{4}\times\mathcal{M}_{3D}\qquad;\qquad X_{4}=K3_{\bot}\times K3_{\|}

(36)

Because the (dimensionless) volume
\mathcal{V}_{{\small X}_{{\small 4}}}

of the CY4 is required to be finite, it follows that the
EFT
3D
,
having four unbroken supercharges, captures remarkable aspects; in
particular
\left(i\right)
a classical topological 3D gravity with non
trival contributions induced by quantum effects;
\left(ii\right)
a
weak/strong gauge duality due to the transposition of the role of the two K3
factors making the Calabi-Yau fourfold
X_{4}
; and
\left(iii\right)

we can effectively test of the asymptotic weak gravity conjecture in both the weak/strong dual pictures.

In the first subsection
3.1,
we recall useful aspects on the
compactification of M-theory on X
4
; this compactification has been
extensively studied in literature; we cite for example
24
;
25A
;
25AB
, hence we will only be interested in the notions that we will
use in our study. In the second subsection
3.2
, we study the gauge
coupling regimes in our
EFT
3D
descending from the
compactification on
K3_{\bot}\times K3_{\|}
. Here, we give new
results on weak and strong gauge regimes in the large and the short distance
limits.

3.1
Compactifying 11D supergravity down to 3D

The action
\mathcal{S}_{11D}
of the eleven dimensional supergravity theory
has 11D gravity field
g_{MN}
, a 3-form gauge potential
\boldsymbol{C}_{3}

and fermionic partners. Using the 4-form field strength
\boldsymbol{F}_{4}=d\boldsymbol{C}_{3}
and restricting to bosonic fields, the leading bosonic
terms in the field action read as follows

\mathcal{S}_{11D}=\frac{2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}{{l}_{11d}^{9}}\int_{\mathcal{M}_{11D}}\left(\mathcal{R}_{11}\ast\mathbf{1}\right)-\frac{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}{{l}_{11d}^{7}}\int_{\mathcal{M}_{11D}}\boldsymbol{F}_{4}\wedge\ast(\boldsymbol{F}_{4})-\frac{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}{6{l}_{11}^{6}}\int_{\mathcal{M}_{11D}}\boldsymbol{C}_{3}\wedge\boldsymbol{F}_{4}\wedge\boldsymbol{F}_{4}

(37)

with 2
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}_{11}^{2}=\left(2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\right)^{8}/M_{11}^{9}
and
{l}_{11d}={M}_{11d}^{-1}.
Under the compactification on
X_{4},
the
3-form potential
\boldsymbol{C}_{3}
decomposes in terms of the 2-form
Kahler generators {
J_{\text{{a}}}
} of the cohomology group
H^{1,1}
(
X_{4}
) like

\boldsymbol{C}_{3}=\frac{1}{2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}l_{11d}}\sum_{\text{{a}}=1}^{h_{{\small X_{4}}}^{1,1}}A_{1}^{\text{{a}}}\wedge J_{\text{{a}}}+...\qquad,\qquad\boldsymbol{F}_{4}=\frac{1}{2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}\sum_{\text{{a}}=1}^{h_{{\small X_{4}}}^{1,1}}F_{2}^{\text{{a}}}\wedge J_{\text{{a}}}+...

(38)

where the
A_{1}^{\text{{a}}}
s are 1-form gauge potentials in 3D
space time given ( in
2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}l_{11d}
units) by

A_{1}^{\text{{a}}}=\displaystyle\int\nolimits_{\mathfrak{C}^{\text{{a}}}}\boldsymbol{C}_{3}\qquad,\qquad\displaystyle\int\nolimits_{\mathfrak{C}^{\text{{a}}}}J_{\text{{a}}}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{\text{{b}}}^{\text{{a}}}

(39)

These are abelian 1-form gauge potentials of the
EFT
3D
with
gauge symmetry

G_{{\small abelian}}=\mathop{\displaystyle\prod}\limits_{\text{{a}}=1}^{h_{{\small X_{4}}}^{1,1}}U\left(1\right)^{\text{{a}}}

(40)

So, under compactification of M-theory on
X_{4}
, the leading bosonic block
terms in the induced 3D effective field action
\mathcal{S}_{3D}
is a
functional of the scalar curvature
\mathcal{R}
of the 3D space-time
\mathcal{M}_{3D}
and the 2-form gauge field strengths
F^{\text{{a}}}=dA^{\text{{a}}}.
This field action has the structure

\mathcal{S}_{3D}=\frac{M_{Pl}}{2}\int_{\mathcal{M}_{3D}}\left(\mathcal{R}\ast\mathbf{1-}\mathfrak{g}_{\text{{xy}}}d{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}}^{\text{{x}}}\wedge\ast d{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}}^{\text{{y}}}\right)-\frac{1}{\mathrm{4}g_{3D}^{2}}\int_{\mathcal{M}_{3D}}G_{\text{{ab}}}\left(F^{\text{{a}}}\wedge\ast F^{\text{{b}}}\right)+...

(41)

where the gauge coupling metric
\mathcal{G}_{\text{{ab}}}
is a
dimensionless quantity ([
\mathcal{G}_{\text{{ab}}}]=Mass^{0}
) given
by

\mathcal{G}_{\text{{ab}}}=\frac{1}{\mathcal{V}_{{\small CY}_{4}}}\int_{{\small CY}_{4}}J_{\text{{a}}}\wedge(\ast J_{\text{{b}}})

(42)

The 3D Planck mass reads in terms of the volume of the
X_{4}
as:

M_{\mathrm{Pl}}=\mathrm{4{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}M_{11}^{9}\mathcal{V}_{{\small CY}_{4}}\qquad\Leftrightarrow\qquad\mathcal{V}_{{\small CY}_{4}}=\frac{M_{\mathrm{Pl}}}{\mathrm{4{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}M_{11}^{9}}\qquad\Leftrightarrow\qquad\mathcal{V}_{{\small X}_{4}}=\frac{M_{\mathrm{Pl}}}{\mathrm{4{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}M_{11}}

(43)

and the 3D gauge coupling is related to the 3D Planck mass
M_{\mathrm{Pl}}

and the
M_{11}^{2}
like

\frac{1}{g_{3D}^{2}}=\frac{\mathrm{M}_{Pl}}{4{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}^{2}M_{11}^{2}}\qquad\Leftrightarrow\qquad\frac{1}{g_{3D}^{2}}=\frac{\mathcal{V}_{{\small X}_{4}}}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}M_{11}}

(44)

it scales like mass; that is
[g_{3D}^{2}]=\mathrm{Mass}^{1}.

We also define the following useful quantities:
\left(1\right)
the
partial dimensional volumes

\mathcal{V}_{\text{{a}}}=\frac{1}{3!}\displaystyle\int\nolimits_{\boldsymbol{D}_{\text{{a}}}}J^{3}\quad,\quad\mathcal{V}_{\text{{ab}}}=\frac{1}{2!}\displaystyle\int\nolimits_{\boldsymbol{S}_{\text{{ab}}}}J^{2}\quad,\quad\mathcal{V}_{\text{{abc}}}=\displaystyle\int\nolimits_{\boldsymbol{C}_{\text{{abc}}}}J

(45)

reading also as

\mathcal{V}_{\text{{a}}}

=

\frac{1}{6}\displaystyle\int\nolimits_{{\small CY}_{{\small 4}}}J_{\text{{a}}}\wedge J^{3}

\mathcal{V}_{\text{{ab}}}

=

\frac{1}{2}\displaystyle\int\nolimits_{{\small CY}_{{\small 4}}}J_{\text{{a}}}\wedge J_{\text{{b}}}\wedge J^{2}

\mathcal{V}_{\text{{abc}}}

=

\displaystyle\int\nolimits_{{\small CY}_{{\small 4}}}J_{\text{{a}}}\wedge J_{\text{{b}}}\wedge J_{\text{{c}}}\wedge J

(46)

They are related to the parameters
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{\text{{a}}}
and the
coupling tensor
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}_{\text{{abcd}}}
like

\mathcal{V}_{\text{{a}}}

=

\frac{1}{6}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}_{\text{{abcd}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{\text{{b}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{\text{{c}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{\text{{d}}}

\mathcal{V}_{\text{{ab}}}

=

\frac{1}{2}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}_{\text{{abcd}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{\text{{c}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{\text{{d}}}

\mathcal{V}_{\text{{abc}}}

=

{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}_{\text{{abcd}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{\text{{d}}}

(47)

with the properties

{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{\text{{a}}}\mathcal{V}_{\text{{a}}}

=

4\mathcal{V}_{{\small CY}_{{\small 4}}}

{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{\text{{a}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{\text{{b}}}\mathcal{V}_{\text{{ab}}}

=

12\mathcal{V}_{{\small CY}_{{\small 4}}}

{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{\text{{a}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{\text{{b}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{\text{{c}}}\mathcal{V}_{\text{{abc}}}

=

24\mathcal{V}_{{\small CY}_{{\small 4}}}

(48)

\left(2\right)
their associated dimensionless partners are given by:

\hat{u}_{\text{{a}}}=\frac{\mathcal{V}_{\text{{a}}}}{\mathcal{V}^{3/4}}\quad,\qquad\hat{u}_{\text{{ab}}}=\frac{\mathcal{V}_{\text{{ab}}}}{\mathcal{V}^{2/4}}\quad,\qquad\hat{u}_{\text{{abc}}}=\frac{\mathcal{V}_{\text{{abc}}}}{\mathcal{V}^{1/4}}

(49)

By setting
\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{a}}}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{\text{{a}}}/\mathcal{V}_{{\small CY}_{{\small 4}}}^{1/4},
we have the properties

\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{a}}}\hat{u}_{\text{{a}}}

=

4

\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{a}}}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{b}}}\hat{u}_{\text{{ab}}}

=

12

\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{a}}}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{b}}}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{c}}}\hat{u}_{\text{{abc}}}

=

24

(50)

The metric
\mathcal{G}_{\text{{ab}}}
(
42
) plays an important
role in the physical description of the 3D effective theory. Being a
dimensionless quantity, this rank 2 tensor (
h_{{\small X_{4}}}^{1,1}\times h_{{\small X_{4}}}^{1,1}
symmetric matrix) can be decomposed in terms of
dimensionless volumes
\hat{u}_{\text{{a}}}
and
\hat{u}_{\text{{ab}}}
of the 6- cycles
\boldsymbol{D}_{\text{{a}}}
and the
4-cycles
\boldsymbol{S}_{\text{{ab}}}
in the
X_{4}
like

\mathcal{G}_{\text{{ab}}}=\hat{u}_{\text{{a}}}\hat{u}_{\text{{b}}}-\hat{u}_{\text{{ab}}}

(51)

Before proceeding notice the three following:

•

The volumes in eq(
45
) have the scale dimensions

[\mathcal{V}_{{\small CY}_{{\small 4}}}]={L}^{8}\quad,\quad[\mathcal{V}_{\text{{a}}}]={L}^{6}\quad,\quad[\mathcal{V}_{\text{{ab}}}]={L}^{4}\quad,\quad[\mathcal{V}_{\text{{abc}}}]={L}^{2}

(52)

but
[\mathcal{V}_{{\small X}_{{\small 4}}}]={L}^{0};
the letter
{L}\
refers to
\emph{Length=1/Mass}
.

•

Being a real symmetric matrix, the
\mathcal{G}_{\text{{ab}}}
(
51
) can be diagonalised by an orthogonal transformation matrix
\mathcal{O}
; thus allowing to put it into the form
25AC
;
25AD

\mathcal{G}_{\text{{ab}}}^{diag}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 280$}}}_{\text{{a}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{\text{{ab}}}\qquad,\qquad\frac{1}{g^{2}}\mathcal{G}_{\text{{ab}}}^{diag}=\frac{1}{g_{\text{{a}}}^{2}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{\text{{ab}}}\qquad,\qquad\frac{1}{g_{\text{{a}}}^{2}}=\frac{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 280$}}}_{\text{{a}}}}{g^{2}}

(53)

with some positive eigenvalues
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 280$}}}_{\text{{a}}}
. In this relation,
we have replaced
g_{3D}
simply by
g
to avoid confusing notations.

Regarding the weak gauge regime in M-theory on
X_{4}
, the Asymptotic WGC
probes weakly coupled directions in the charge lattice of
X_{4}
. These
charge directions are closely related to the gauge kinetic matrix
\mathcal{G}_{\text{{ab}}}
presented above. In fact, to have a weakly coupled
gauge field
A_{1}
in the 3D effective field theory descending from
M-theory on
X_{4}
, we proceed as follows:

First, we think about the 1-form gauge potential
A_{1}
in terms of a
linear combination of individual 3D gauge potentials like
A_{1}=\sum n_{\text{{a}}}A_{1}^{\text{{a}}}
with some integers
n_{\text{{a}}}.
By using (
38
), in particular the expression of
A_{1}^{\text{{a}}}
in terms of the 3-form potential
\boldsymbol{C}_{3}

namely

A_{1}^{\text{{a}}}=\displaystyle\int\nolimits_{\mathcal{C}^{\text{{a}}}}\boldsymbol{C}_{3}\qquad,\qquad\displaystyle\int\nolimits_{\mathcal{C}^{\text{{a}}}}J_{\text{{b}}}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{\text{{b}}}^{\text{{a}}}

(54)

with basis curves
\mathcal{C}^{\text{{a}}}
dual to the divisors
J_{\text{{a}}}
; it results that the gauge potential
A_{1}=\sum_{\text{{a}}}n_{\text{{a}}}A_{1}^{\text{{a}}}
is in fact given
by the compactification of
\boldsymbol{C}_{3}
over a complex curve
\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{n}}=\sum_{\text{{a}}}n_{\text{{a}}}\mathcal{C}^{\text{{a}}}
with integer vector
\mathbf{n=(}n_{1},...,n_{h_{{\small X}_{{\small 4}}}^{1,1}}\mathbf{)}.
Explicitly, this

A_{1}
may be denoted like

A_{1}\left[\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{n}}\right]=\displaystyle\int\nolimits_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{n}}}\boldsymbol{C}_{3}

(55)

which by substituting
\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{n}}=\sum n_{\text{{a}}}\mathcal{C}^{\text{{a}}}
, it expands as follows

A_{1}\left[\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{n}}\right]=\sum_{\text{{a}}}n_{\text{{a}}}A_{1}\left[\mathcal{C}^{\text{{a}}}\right]\qquad,\qquad A_{1}\left[\mathcal{C}^{\text{{a}}}\right]=\displaystyle\int\nolimits_{\mathcal{C}^{\text{{a}}}}\boldsymbol{C}_{3}

(56)

Naively, the weak gauge coupling in the infinite distance limit (
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow\infty
) corresponds to

g_{\text{{a}}}^{2}\rightarrow 0

(57)

As is obvious from the label
a,
this would depend on the curves in
the basis that we take into account; we will see later that these curves
should lie entirely in the fiber (resp. in the base) in order to
obtain the weak coupling condition (resp. strong coupling condition). Also this behaviour was shown to be only a necessary condition for a more
constrained condition. Both these aspects are studied in the next
subsection.

3.2
Gauge coupling regimes in M-theory on
K3_{\bot}\times K3_{\|}

In this subsection, we investigate conditions for weak (resp. strong) gauge
coupling regimes in the large distance limit
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow\infty
(
resp. short distance
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow 0
) in the Kahler moduli space
\mathfrak{M}_{{\small CY}_{{\small 4}}}
for the fibration

{\small CY}_{{\small 4}}=K3_{\bot}\times K3_{\|}

(58)

In other words, we want to look for regimes
\mathrm{g}_{\mathrm{YM}}^{weak}

and
\mathrm{g}_{\mathrm{YM}}^{strong}
of the effective gauge coupling
constants
\mathrm{g}_{\mathrm{YM}}
in the 3D gauge theory descending from
M-theory on
X_{4}.
Obviously, it is the weak coupling regime
\mathrm{g}_{\mathrm{YM}}^{weak}
which is important for perturbative QFT
3D
; but
here we want to also explore relevant aspects on the strong regime of
\mathrm{g}_{\mathrm{YM}}^{strong}
in relationship with the mapping

\mathrm{g}_{\mathrm{YM}}^{weak}\qquad\rightarrow\qquad\frac{1}{\mathrm{g}_{\mathrm{YM}}^{weak}}\sim\mathrm{g}_{\mathrm{YM}}^{strong}

(59)

To that purpose, we begin by introducing useful tools on the weak gravity
conjecture (WGC) as well as on the asymptotic weak coupling regime
compatible with WGC. After that, we turn to investigate with details the
weak (resp. strong) regimes of the gauge couplings (
\mathrm{g}_{\mathrm{YM}}^{weak}
/
\mathrm{g}_{\mathrm{YM}}^{strong}
) by using underlying U(1)
Υ
gauge coupling constant
\mathrm{g}_{\Upsilon}
with
\Upsilon
labelling a complex curve in the
X_{4}.

3.2.1
Magnetic weak gauge coupling scale
\Lambda_{\text{{wgc}}}

We start by describing the relationships defining the weak gravity
conjecture in D- dimensional space time in the framework of M-theory on
Calabi-Yau manifolds with finite volume (
\mathcal{V}_{{\small CY}_{{\small 11-2D}}}<\infty
). In D- dimensional gravity theory coupled to a U(1) gauge
potential with gauge coupling
g_{{}_{U(1)}},
the WGC stipulates that there
should exist at least one massive charged particle state
\left|m,Q\right\rangle
with mass m and electric charge
Q=qg_{{}_{U(1)}}
(q
integer) satisfying the inequality

m\leq qg_{{}_{U(1)}}M_{\mathrm{Pl}}^{\frac{D-2}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{D-2}{D-3}}+....

(60)

Here, the
M_{\mathrm{Pl}}
is the D- dimensional Planck mass related to Newton constant like
G_{N}\sim M_{\mathrm{Pl}}^{2-D}
. Such a
particle state is often called a super-extremal particle due to the
formulation of the WGC using extremal black holes
7
;
ZX
;
ZY
;
ZXY
. Together with this electric WGC condition, we also have a magnetic WGC
relations given by

\left.\Lambda_{{}_{\mathrm{WGC}}}^{2}\right|_{{}_{U(1)}}=g_{{}_{U(1)}}^{2}M_{\mathrm{Pl}}^{D-2}

(61)

In
14A
, it was shown that the naive condition (
57
)
is not enough to describe the weak coupling. In fact, the true weak gauge
coupling is defined as

\lim_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow\infty}\left(\frac{\left.\Lambda_{{}_{\mathrm{WGC}}}^{2}\right|_{{}_{U(1)}}}{\Lambda_{{}_{\mathrm{QG}}}^{2}}\right)=0

(62)

where
\left.\Lambda_{{}_{\mathrm{WGC}}}^{2}\right|_{{}_{U(1)}}
is the
magnetic WGC scale associated with the U(1) gauge symmetry. The
\Lambda_{{}_{\mathrm{WGC}}}
scales like a mass (
\left[\Lambda_{{}_{\mathrm{WGC}}}\right]=Mass^{1}
); and the
g_{{}_{U(1)}}^{2}
scales as,

[g_{{}_{U(1)}}^{2}]=\left(Length\right)^{D-4}\qquad\Leftrightarrow\qquad[g_{{}_{U(1)}}^{2}]=\left(Mass\right)^{4-D}

(63)

The energy scale
\Lambda_{{}_{\mathrm{QG}}}
in eq(
62
) is a quantum
gravity cut-off given by an energy level
\mathcal{E}_{{}_{\mathrm{QG}}}

above which the gravitational coupling becomes strong; and thus the dynamics
of gravity changes in such a way that the description of the quantum theory
is no more consistent. Given the magnetic WGC relations (
61
-
62
) that we rewrite for a given
U(1)_{\text{{a}}}
, with a fixed label

a
=1,...,h_{{\small CY}_{4}}^{1,1},
as follows

\left.\Lambda_{{}_{\mathrm{WGC}}}^{2}\right|_{{}_{U(1)_{\text{{a}}}}}=g_{{}_{U(1)_{\text{{a}}}}}^{2}M_{\mathrm{Pl}}^{D-2}\qquad,\qquad\lim_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow\infty}\left(\frac{\left.\Lambda_{{}_{\mathrm{WGC}}}^{2}\right|_{{}_{U(1)_{\text{{a}}}}}}{\Lambda_{{}_{\mathrm{QG}}}^{2}}\right)=0

(64)

Thus, we see that the condition of weak coupling limit
g_{{}_{U(1)_{\text{{a}}}}}^{2}\rightarrow 0
derived in the previous subsection is
merely a necessary condition. The full condition needs to include the
quantum gravity cut-off to find the true weakly coupled directions.

A. Extending eqs(
61
-
62
) to
U(1)_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}}:

Here, we shall think about eqs(
61
-
62
) in terms of a generic
gauge symmetry
U(1)_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}}
[and 1-form gauge field
A_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}}
associated with
U(1)_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}}
]. This abelian gauge symmetry
U(1)_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}}
is a subgroup
of
H_{\mathrm{r}_{\mathrm{g}}}=U(1)^{\mathrm{r}_{\mathrm{g}}}
with
\mathrm{r}_{\mathrm{g}}=h_{{\small X}_{{\small 4}}}^{1,1}
; it is
characterised by an integral charge vector
\mathbf{q}=(q_{1},...,q_{\mathrm{r}_{\mathrm{g}}});
the Lie algebra
u(1)_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}}

associated with this gauge symmetry is given by the following linear
combination

u(1)_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}}=\sum_{\text{{a}}}q_{\text{{a}}}u(1)_{C^{\text{{a}}}}\qquad,\qquad A_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}}=\sum_{\text{{a}}}q_{\text{{a}}}\mathfrak{A}_{C^{\text{{a}}}}

(65)

where, for later use, we set

u(1)_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}}\equiv u(1)_{\mathbf{q}}\qquad,\qquad u(1)_{C^{\text{{a}}}}\equiv u(1)^{\text{{a}}}\qquad,\qquad\mathfrak{A}_{C^{\text{{a}}}}\equiv\mathfrak{A}^{\text{{a}}}

(66)

By using
\left(i\right)
the commuting Cartan-like generators {
\mathfrak{h}_{\text{{a}}}
} of the abelian
H_{\mathrm{r}_{\mathrm{g}}},
and
\left(ii\right)
the factorisation

H_{\mathrm{r}_{\mathrm{g}}}=U(1)_{\mathbf{q}}\times H^{\vee}\qquad,\qquad H^{\vee}\equiv\frac{H_{\mathrm{r}_{\mathrm{g}}}}{U(1)_{\mathbf{q}}}

(67)

the abelian factor
U(1)_{\mathbf{q}}
is generated by a diagonal generator
h_{\mathbf{t}}^{0}
given by a linear combination of the diagonal {
\mathfrak{h}_{\text{{a}}}
} of like

h_{\mathbf{T}_{0}}=\sum_{\text{{a}}=0}^{\mathrm{r}_{\mathrm{g}}-1}t_{0}^{\text{{a}}}\mathfrak{h}_{\text{{a}}}

(68)

with parameters
\mathbf{T}_{0}=(t_{0}^{0},t_{0}^{1},...,t_{0}^{\mathrm{r}_{\mathrm{g}}-1})
. The other
\mathrm{r}_{\mathrm{g}}-1
diagonal generators
h_{\mathbf{T}_{0}}
generating the coset group
H^{\vee}=H_{\mathrm{r}_{\mathrm{g}}}/U(1)_{\mathbf{q}}
expands like

h_{\mathbf{T}_{a}}=\sum_{\text{{a}}=0}^{\mathrm{r}_{\mathrm{g}}}t_{a}^{\text{{a}}}\mathfrak{h}_{\text{{a}}}\qquad,\qquad a=1,...,\mathrm{r}_{\mathrm{g}}-1

(69)

If combining
t^{\text{{a}}}
and
t_{a}^{\text{{a}}}
into a
\mathrm{r}_{\mathrm{g}}\times\mathrm{r}_{\mathrm{g}}
charge matrix
T_{\text{{a}}}^{\text{{b}}},
we have

h_{\mathbf{t}_{\text{{a}}}}=\sum_{\text{{b}}=0}^{\mathrm{r}_{\mathrm{g}}-1}T_{\text{{a}}}^{\text{{b}}}\mathfrak{h}_{\text{{b}}}\qquad with\qquad\det(T_{\text{{a}}}^{\text{{b}}})\neq 0

(70)

In what follows, we will be interested by the particular abelian
U(1)_{\mathbf{q}}
and the associated gauge field potential
A_{\mathbf{q}}

given by the linear combination

A_{\mathbf{q}}=\sum_{\text{{a}}=0}^{\mathrm{r}_{\mathrm{g}}-1}q_{\text{{a}}}\mathfrak{A}^{\text{{a}}}\qquad with\qquad\mathfrak{A}^{\text{{a}}}=\mathfrak{A}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}}^{\text{{a}}}dx^{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}}

(71)

For this particular
U(1)_{\mathbf{q}}
abelian gauge symmetry, the
conditions (
61
-
62
) for the
magnetic
WGC reads as
follows,

\left.\Lambda_{{}_{\mathrm{WGC}}}^{2}\right|_{{}_{U(1)_{\mathbf{q}}}}=g_{{}_{U(1)_{\mathbf{q}}}}^{2}M_{\mathrm{Pl}}^{D-2}

(72)

and

\lim_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow\infty}\left(\frac{\left.\Lambda_{{}_{\mathrm{WGC}}}^{2}\right|_{{}_{U(1)_{\mathbf{q}}}}}{\Lambda_{{}_{\mathrm{QG}}}^{2}}\right)=0

(73)

In this relation, the squared gauge coupling
g_{{}_{U(1)_{\mathbf{q}}}}^{2}

is quadratic in the partial gauge coupling
g_{\text{{a}}}

associated with the individual gauge group factors
U(1)_{q_{\text{{a}}}}\equiv U(1)_{\text{{a}}};
it reads explicitly like

g_{{}_{U(1)_{\mathbf{q}}}}^{2}=\mathrm{g}_{\text{{a}}}\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}\mathrm{g}_{\text{{b}}}

(74)

where
\mathrm{g}_{\text{{a}}}=q_{\text{{a}}}\mathrm{g}_{{}_{\mathrm{YM}}}
and where
\mathrm{g}_{{}_{\mathrm{YM}}}
is Yang-Mills
coupling; the usual non abelian gauge constant of the underlying D-
dimensional effective field theory with gauge invariance
\boldsymbol{G}_{\mathrm{r}_{\mathrm{g}}}
. The
\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}
is the
inverse of the effective gauge coupling metric
\mathcal{G}_{\text{{ab}}}
(
42
) involved in the field action (
41
).

B. Weak gravity conjecture in 3D:

In three space time dimensions, the magnetic WGC relation (
61
) reads
like
\left.\Lambda_{{}_{\mathrm{WGC}}}^{2}\right|_{{}_{U(1)_{\mathbf{q}}}}=g_{{}_{U(1)_{\mathbf{q}}}}^{2}M_{\mathrm{Pl}}
with the squared
g_{{}_{U(1)_{\mathbf{q}}}}^{2}
expressed as follows

\mathrm{g}_{{}_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}}}^{2}=g_{3D}^{2}\left(q_{\text{{a}}}\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}q_{\text{{b}}}\right)

(75)

the scaling dimension being
[\mathrm{g}_{\text{{a}}}^{2}]=[g_{3D}^{2}]=Length^{-1}
; the couplings
\mathrm{g}_{\text{{a}}}
and
g_{3D}
have a mass dimension. Notice also the two following:
\left(\mathbf{i}\right)
by substituting (
75
) into
\left.\Lambda_{{}_{\mathrm{WGC}}}^{2}\right|_{{}_{U(1)_{\mathbf{q}}}}=g_{{}_{U(1)_{\mathbf{q}}}}^{2}M_{\mathrm{Pl}},
we obtain

\left.\Lambda_{{}_{\mathrm{WGC}}}^{2}\right|_{{}_{u(1)_{\mathbf{q}}}}=g_{3D}^{2}\left(q_{\text{{a}}}\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}q_{\text{{b}}}\right)M_{\mathrm{Pl}}

(76)

this is a function of the gauge coupling constant
g_{3D}
but also on the
charges
q_{\text{{a}}}
and the inverse of the metric of the moduli
space of the 3D effective gauge theory.
\left(\mathbf{ii}\right)
Putting
into (
72
), the weak gravity conjecture reads like

\lim_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow\infty}\left(\frac{g_{3D}^{2}M_{\mathrm{Pl}}\left(q_{\text{{a}}}\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}q_{\text{{b}}}\right)}{\Lambda_{{}_{\mathrm{QG}}}^{2}}\right)=0

(77)

To illustrate the different regimes of the gauge coupling, we consider the
example of M-theory on
X_{4}
in the infinite distance limit of Type-
\mathbb{S}
, with an emphasis on type K3
25B
;
25C
; that is a
X_{4}
given by K3 surface fibered over a complex base surface
\mathbb{B}_{2}
. This surface
\mathbb{B}_{2}
can be imagined as one of the
familiar surfaces like del Pezzo surfaces dP
{}_{n},
given by the blow up of
\mathbb{P}^{2}
at n points (
n\leq 8
)
25D
;
25DA
;
25DB
, or
the Hirzebruch surfaces
\mathbb{F}_{n}
based on the fibration
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}

25E
;
25EA
. For
illustration and in order to have quite simple calculations, we give below a
toy model where the base
\mathbb{B}_{2}
is taken as
another K3 surface, this choice turns out to exhibit some interesting
features.

3.2.2
Gauge coupling regimes in M-theory on
K3_{\bot}\times K3_{\|}

We have seen that a necessary condition of the weak coupling
g_{{}_{\Upsilon}}^{2}=g_{3D}^{2}(q_{\text{{a}}}\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}q_{\text{{b}}})
in the infinite distance limit (
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow\infty
) that is associated with some complex curve
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}

and gauge symmetry
U(1)_{\mathbf{q}}
as

\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}=\sum_{\text{{a}}=1}^{h_{X_{4}}^{1,1}}q_{\text{{a}}}\mathcal{C}^{\text{{a}}}\qquad\leftrightarrow\qquad u(1)_{\mathbf{q}}=\sum_{\text{{a}}=1}^{h_{X_{4}}^{1,1}}q_{\text{{a}}}u(1)^{\text{{a}}}\qquad,\qquad u(1)_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}}\equiv u(1)_{\mathbf{q}}

(78)

is given by

\lim_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow\infty}\left[g_{3D}^{2}(q_{\text{{a}}}\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}q_{\text{{b}}})\right]=0

(79)

Because
\mathcal{G}_{\text{{ab}}}
defined by eq(
42
) is a
function of the Kahler form
J,
which depends on the parameter
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}},

it follows that
\mathcal{G}_{\text{{ab}}}
and its inverse
\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}
are also functions of
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}.
Moreover,
expressing (
24
) giving the asymptotic expansion of the Kahler 2-form
J
for the case of
X_{4}=K3_{\perp}\times K3_{\parallel}
like

J={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}J_{\parallel}+\frac{1}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}J_{\perp}

(80)

with

J_{\parallel}=\sum_{a_{\parallel}=1}^{h_{\parallel}^{1,1}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{a_{\parallel}}J_{a_{\parallel}}\qquad,\qquad J_{\perp}=\sum_{i_{\bot}=1}^{h_{\perp}^{1,1}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{i_{\bot}}J_{i_{\bot}}

(81)

where we have set
h_{\parallel}^{1,1}=h^{1,1}(K3_{\parallel})
and
h_{\perp}^{1,1}=h^{1,1}(K3_{\perp}),
it results that
\mathcal{G}_{\text{{ab}}}
can be splitted like

\mathcal{G}_{\text{{ab}}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathcal{G}_{\parallel\parallel}&\mathcal{G}_{\parallel\perp}\\
\mathcal{G}_{\perp\parallel}&\mathcal{G}_{\perp\perp}\end{array}\right),\qquad\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathcal{G}^{\parallel\parallel}&\mathcal{G}^{\parallel\perp}\\
\mathcal{G}^{\perp\parallel}&\mathcal{G}^{\perp\perp}\end{array}\right)

(82)

with

\mathcal{G}_{\parallel\parallel}

=

\hat{u}_{\parallel}\hat{u}_{\parallel}-\hat{u}_{\parallel\parallel}

\mathcal{G}_{\perp\perp}

=

\hat{u}_{\perp}\hat{u}_{\perp}-\hat{u}_{\perp\perp}

\mathcal{G}_{\parallel\perp}

=

\hat{u}_{\parallel}\hat{u}_{\perp}-\hat{u}_{\parallel\perp}

(83)

Notice the following useful features:
\left(\mathbf{1}\right)
the
inverse
\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}
in terms of the components of
\mathcal{G}_{\text{{ab}}}
can be formally exhibited as follows

\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{\mathcal{G}_{\perp\perp}}{\mathcal{G}_{\parallel\parallel}\mathcal{G}_{\perp\perp}-\mathcal{G}_{\parallel\perp}\mathcal{G}_{\perp\parallel}}&-\frac{\mathcal{G}_{\parallel\perp}}{\mathcal{G}_{\parallel\parallel}\mathcal{G}_{\perp\perp}-\mathcal{G}_{\parallel\perp}\mathcal{G}^{\perp\parallel}}\\
-\frac{\mathcal{G}_{\perp\parallel}}{\mathcal{G}_{\parallel\parallel}\mathcal{G}_{\perp\perp}-\mathcal{G}_{\parallel\perp}\mathcal{G}_{\perp\parallel}}&\frac{\mathcal{G}_{\parallel\parallel}}{\mathcal{G}_{\parallel\parallel}\mathcal{G}_{\perp\perp}-\mathcal{G}_{\parallel\perp}\mathcal{G}_{\perp\parallel}}\end{array}\right)

(84)

from which we see that in the infinite distance limit the
\mathcal{G}^{\parallel\parallel}
behaves as the one of
\mathcal{G}_{\perp\perp}
,
and the
\mathcal{G}^{\perp\perp}
behaves as the one of
\mathcal{G}_{\parallel\parallel}.
As shown below, the behaviours for
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow\infty
read as follows

\mathcal{G}_{\bot\bot}

\sim

{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{+2}\mathring{G}_{\bot\bot}

\qquad,\qquad

\mathcal{G}^{\bot\bot}

\sim

{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{-2}\mathring{G}^{\bot\bot}

\mathcal{G}_{\bot\|}

\sim

{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{0}\mathring{G}_{\bot\|}

\qquad,\qquad

\mathcal{G}^{\bot\|}

\sim

{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{0}\mathring{G}^{\bot\|}

\mathcal{G}_{\|\|}

\sim

{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{-2}\mathring{G}_{\|\|}

\qquad,\qquad

\mathcal{G}^{\|\|}

\sim

{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{+2}\mathring{G}^{\|\|}

\mathcal{G}_{\|\bot}

\sim

{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{0}\mathring{G}_{\|\bot}

\qquad,\qquad

\mathcal{G}^{\|\bot}

\sim

{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{0}\mathring{G}^{\|\bot}

(85)
