\hat{u}_{\bot\parallel}

=

\frac{1}{2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}_{{}_{\bot\|\bot\bot}}\left(\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\bot}\right)^{2}+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}_{{}_{\bot\|\|\bot}}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\|}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\bot}+\frac{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}}{2}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}_{{}_{\bot\|\|\|}}(\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\|})^{2}

(87)

Depending on the value of
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}},
we distinguish the behaviors:

•

Infinite distance limit
(
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow\infty
):

\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\hat{u}_{\parallel}$&$\simeq$&$\frac{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{3}}{6}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}_{{}_{\|\|\|\|}}(\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\|})^{3}$\\
$\hat{u}_{\parallel\parallel}$&$\simeq$&$\frac{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}}{2}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}_{{}_{\|\|\|\|}}(\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\|})^{2}$\\
$\hat{u}_{\parallel\bot}$&$\simeq$&$\frac{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}}{2}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}_{{}_{\|\bot\|\|}}(\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\|})^{2}$\end{tabular}\qquad,\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\hat{u}_{\perp}$&$\simeq$&$\frac{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{3}}{6}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}_{{}_{\bot\|\|\|}}(\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\|})^{3}$\\
$\hat{u}_{\bot\bot}$&$\simeq$&$\frac{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}}{2}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}_{{}_{\bot\bot\|\|}}(\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\|})^{2}$\\
$\hat{u}_{\bot\parallel}$&$\simeq$&$\frac{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}}{2}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}_{{}_{\bot\|\|\|}}(\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\|})^{2}$\end{tabular}

(88)

•

Short distance limit
(
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow 0
):

\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\hat{u}_{\parallel}$&$\simeq$&$\frac{1}{6{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{3}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}_{{}_{\|\bot\bot\bot}}\left(\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\bot}\right)^{3}\ \ $\\
$\hat{u}_{\parallel\parallel}$&$\simeq$&$\frac{1}{2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}_{{}_{\|\|\bot\bot}}\left(\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\bot}\right)^{2}\ $\\
$\hat{u}_{\parallel\bot}$&$\simeq$&$\frac{1}{2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}_{{}_{\|\bot\bot\bot}}\left(\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\bot}\right)^{2}\ $\end{tabular}\qquad,\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\hat{u}_{\perp}$&$\simeq$&$\frac{1}{6{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{3}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}_{{}_{\bot\bot\bot\bot}}\left(\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\bot}\right)^{3}$\\
$\hat{u}_{\bot\bot}$&$\simeq$&$\frac{1}{2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}_{{}_{\bot\bot\bot\bot}}\left(\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\bot}\right)^{2}$\\
$\hat{u}_{\bot\parallel}$&$\simeq$&$\frac{1}{2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}_{{}_{\bot\|\bot\bot}}\left(\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\bot}\right)^{2}$\end{tabular}

(89)

Notice that the above expressions (
88
) and (
89
) are related
under
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow 1/{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}.
Notice also that by this duality,
the basis of curves in
X_{4}
is given by the union of two sets like

\{\mathcal{C}^{\text{{a}}}\}_{X_{4}}=\{\mathcal{C}^{\text{{a}}}\}_{K3_{\parallel}}\quad\mathop{\displaystyle\bigcup}\quad\{\mathcal{C}^{\text{{a}}}\}_{K3_{\perp}}

(90)

with

\{\mathcal{C}^{\text{{a}}}\}_{K3_{\parallel}}

=

\{\mathcal{C}^{a_{\parallel}}\}_{a_{\parallel}=1,...,h_{\parallel}^{1,1}}

\{\mathcal{C}^{\text{{a}}}\}_{K3_{\perp}}

=

\{\mathcal{C}^{i_{\perp}}\}_{i_{\perp}=1,...,h_{\perp}^{1,1}}

(91)

This notation allows to express the expansion of the complex curve
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}
formally like
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\parallel}}+\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\perp}}
with

\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\parallel}}=\sum_{a_{\parallel}=1_{\parallel}}^{h_{\parallel}^{1,1}}q_{a_{\parallel}}\mathcal{C}^{a_{\parallel}}\qquad,\qquad\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\perp}}=\sum_{i_{\perp}=1_{\perp}}^{h_{\perp}^{1,1}}q_{i_{\perp}}\mathcal{C}^{i_{\perp}}

(92)

By focusing on the abelian gauge coupling term
\int_{M_{3D}}\mathcal{G}_{\text{{ab}}}\left(F^{\text{{a}}}\wedge\ast F^{\text{{b}}}\right)
that we expand like

\displaystyle\int\nolimits_{M_{3D}}\mathcal{G}_{\text{{ab}}}\left(F^{\text{{a}}}\wedge\ast F^{\text{{b}}}\right)

=

\displaystyle\int\nolimits_{M_{3D}}\mathcal{G}_{\bot\bot}\left(F^{\bot}\wedge\ast F^{\bot}\right)+\displaystyle\int\nolimits_{M_{3D}}\mathcal{G}_{\bot\|}\left(F^{\bot}\wedge\ast F^{\|}\right)+

\displaystyle\int\nolimits_{M_{3D}}\mathcal{G}_{\|\|}\left(F^{\|}\wedge\ast F^{\|}\right)+\displaystyle\int\nolimits_{M_{3D}}\mathcal{G}_{\|\bot}\left(F^{\|}\wedge\ast F^{\bot}\right)

(93)

and using the infinite distance limit of the coupling metric (
85
)
namely
\mathcal{G}_{\bot\bot}\sim\mathcal{O}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{+2}\right)

and
\mathcal{G}_{\bot\|}\sim\mathcal{O}\left(1\right)
as well as
\mathcal{G}_{\|\|}\sim\mathcal{O}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{-2}\right)
, we
see that (
93
) involves different gauge regimes. Moreover, setting
q_{\text{{a}}}=(q_{a_{\parallel}},q_{i_{\perp}})
and substituting
\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}
as in eq(
82
), we learn that the
effective gauge coupling
g_{{}_{\Upsilon}}^{2}=g_{3D}^{2}(q_{\text{{a}}}\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}q_{\text{{b}}})
splits into three
blocs as follows

g_{{}_{\Upsilon}}^{2}=\left(g_{{}_{\Upsilon}}^{2}\right)_{\parallel\parallel}+2\left(g_{{}_{\Upsilon}}^{2}\right)_{\parallel\perp}+\left(g_{{}_{\Upsilon}}^{2}\right)_{\perp\perp}

(94)

with infinite distance limit behaviours like

\displaystyle\left(g_{{}_{\Upsilon}}^{2}\right)_{\parallel\parallel}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}g_{3D}^{2}(q_{a_{\parallel}}\mathring{G}^{a_{\parallel}b_{\parallel}}q_{b_{\parallel}})

(95)

\displaystyle\left(g_{{}_{\Upsilon}}^{2}\right)_{\parallel\perp}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{0}g_{3D}^{2}(q_{a_{\parallel}}\mathring{G}^{a_{\parallel}i_{\perp}}q_{i_{\perp}})

(96)

\displaystyle\left(g_{{}_{\Upsilon}}^{2}\right)_{\perp\perp}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}}g_{3D}^{2}(q_{i_{\perp}}\mathring{G}^{i_{\perp}j_{\perp}}q_{j_{\perp}})

(97)

showing that for
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow\infty,
we have
\left(g_{{}_{\Upsilon}}^{2}\right)_{\parallel\parallel}\sim\mathcal{O}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}\right)\rightarrow\infty
while
\left(g_{{}_{\Upsilon}}^{2}\right)_{\perp\perp}\sim\mathcal{O}\left(1/{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}\right)\rightarrow 0.

From these behaviours, we see that for fourfold
X_{4}=K3_{\perp}\times K3_{\parallel},
there are two dual gauge regimes.

\left(\mathbf{i}\right)

A weakly coupled direction [
\left(g_{{}_{\Upsilon}}^{2}\right)_{\perp\perp}\rightarrow 0
] associated with
fibral curve (
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\parallel}}\rightarrow 0
). So, the
complex curve
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}
which in general is given by the
expansion (
92
) reduces down to

q_{a_{\parallel}}=0\qquad,\qquad\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\perp}}=\sum q_{i_{\perp}}\mathcal{C}^{i_{\perp}}

(98)

For this curve, the gauge coupling
g_{{}_{\Upsilon_{weak}}}^{2}
vanishes in
the infinite distance limit as shown below

\mathrm{g}_{{}_{\Upsilon_{weak}}}^{2}=g_{3D}^{2}(q_{i_{\perp}}\mathcal{G}^{i_{\perp}j_{\perp}}q_{j_{\perp}})\qquad,\qquad\mathrm{g}_{{}_{\Upsilon_{weak}}}^{2}\sim\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}}\right)\rightarrow 0

(99)

For this U
\left(1\right)_{weak}
gauge symmetry, the corresponding 3D
Maxwell-like field action reads in terms of the field strength
F^{weak}=dA^{weak}
and the coupling constant
g_{{}_{\Upsilon_{weak}}}^{2}

as follows

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\Upsilon_{weak}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4\mathrm{g}_{{}_{\Upsilon_{weak}}}^{2}}\displaystyle\int\nolimits_{M_{3D}}F^{weak}\wedge\ast F^{weak}+

(100)

\displaystyle\frac{M_{\mathrm{Pl}}}{2}\int_{\mathcal{M}_{3D}}\mathcal{R}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathcal{M}_{3D}}\mathfrak{g}_{\text{{xy}}}d{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}}^{\text{{x}}}\wedge\ast d{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}}^{\text{{y}}}

Notice that here the 1-form gauge potential
A^{weak}
is related to the
3-form potential
\boldsymbol{C}_{3}
of the M-theory as follows

A^{weak}=\displaystyle\int\nolimits_{\Upsilon_{weak}}\boldsymbol{C}_{3}

(101)

\left(\mathbf{ii}\right)

A strongly coupled direction [
\left(g_{{}_{\Upsilon}}^{2}\right)_{\parallel\parallel}\rightarrow\infty
]
associated with base curve (
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\perp}}\rightarrow 0
).
Here, the curve
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}
reduces to

q_{i_{\perp}}=0\qquad,\qquad\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\parallel}}=\sum q_{a_{\parallel}}\mathcal{C}^{a_{\parallel}}

(102)

and the gauge coupling
g_{{}_{\Upsilon_{strong}}}^{2}
is given by

\mathrm{g}_{{}_{\Upsilon_{strong}}}^{2}=g_{3D}^{2}(q_{a_{\parallel}}\mathcal{G}^{a_{\parallel}b_{\parallel}}q_{b_{\parallel}})\qquad,\qquad\mathrm{g}_{{}_{\Upsilon_{strong}}}^{2}\sim\mathcal{O}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}\right)\rightarrow\infty

(103)

Similarly for the
\mathcal{S}_{\Upsilon^{weak}}
, here also the
corresponding 3D Maxwell-like field action invariant under the U
\left(1\right)_{strong}
gauge symmetry reads in terms of the field strength
F^{strong}=dA^{strong}
and the coupling constant
g_{{}_{\Upsilon^{strong}}}^{2}
as follows

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\Upsilon_{strong}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4\mathrm{g}_{{}_{\Upsilon_{strong}}}^{2}}\displaystyle\int\nolimits_{M_{3D}}F^{strong}\wedge\ast F^{strong}+

(104)

\displaystyle\frac{M_{\mathrm{Pl}}}{2}\int_{\mathcal{M}_{3D}}\mathcal{R}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathcal{M}_{3D}}\mathfrak{g}_{\text{{xy}}}d{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}}^{\text{{x}}}\wedge\ast d{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}}^{\text{{y}}}

with

A^{strong}=\displaystyle\int\nolimits_{\Upsilon_{strong}}\boldsymbol{C}_{3}

(105)

Notice that for the fourfold
X_{4}=K3_{\perp}\times K3_{\parallel},
the
Hodge number
h_{\parallel}^{1,1}
in the base
K3_{\parallel}
and the
Hodge number
h_{\perp}^{1,1}
of the fiber
K3_{\perp}
are equal; so we
can take

h_{\parallel}^{1,1}=h_{\perp}^{1,1}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}\qquad,\qquad h_{X_{4}}^{1,1}=2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}

(106)

Then, the gauge symmetry (
40
) of the
EFT
3D
is
given by
U(1)^{2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}}
; it factorises like

G_{abelian}=U(1)_{fiber}^{\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}}}\times U(1)_{base}^{\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}}}

(107)

Notice also that because of the automorphism symmetry of
X_{4}
generated
by the permutation,

K3_{\perp}\qquad\leftrightarrow\qquad K3_{\parallel}

(108)

characteristic properties in the fiber
K3_{\perp}
(like weakly coupled
directions) gets mapped to dual properties in the base
K3_{\parallel}

(strongly coupled directions). This feature can be manifestly exhibited in
various ways; for instance by using the mapping
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow 1/{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}
exchanging
\left(g_{{}_{\Upsilon}}^{2}\right)_{\perp\perp}

and
\left(g_{{}_{\Upsilon}}^{2}\right)_{\parallel\parallel}
in eq(
95
-
97
). It reads also from the particular factorisation of the
gauge symmetry (
107
). This phenomenon, to which we refer below to
as
Weak/Strong gauge duality
, is further investigated in the next
section.

4
Weak/Strong gauge duality in 3D

In this section, we deepen the study of the Weak/Strong gauge duality in
M-theory on
X_{4}=K3_{\perp}\times K3_{\parallel}
in connection with the
Asymptotic WGC and the Repulsive Force conjecture (RFC). We first develop
the Type-
\mathbb{S}
form for this
X_{4}
and its Weak/Strong gauge
duality properties induced by the exchange of the role of the fiber and the
base surfaces in
X_{4}
. Then, we investigate the implications of the
X_{4}
automorphism (
108
) generated by the transposition
K3_{\perp}\leftrightarrow K3_{\parallel}
on the Asymptotic WGC. This feature
translates into Weak/Strong gauge duality in the
\emph{EFT}_{{\small 3D}}.
After that, we study the RFC in the
\emph{EFT}_{{\small 3D}}

and give its verification for both towers of BPS and non BPS states.

4.1
Type- surface form for
K3_{\perp}\times K3_{\parallel}

We start by recalling that the non vanishing Hodge numbers
h_{K3_{x}}^{p,q}

of the fibral
K3_{\perp}
and the base
K3_{\parallel}
surfaces namely,

h_{K3_{x}}^{0,0}=h_{K3_{x}}^{2,2}=1,\qquad h_{K3_{x}}^{1,1}=20,\qquad h_{K3_{x}}^{2,1}=h_{K3_{x}}^{1,2}=1

(109)

This indicates that the number
{\large{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}}_{\mathcal{C}_{2}}
of 2-cycles

\mathcal{C}_{2}
in the fibered CY4 is less than or equal to 40 (
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}_{\mathcal{C}_{2}}\leq h_{X_{4}}^{1,1}=40).
So, the parametrisation of the
Kahler 2-form (
80
-
81
) is generated at most by the 20 two-forms
J_{a_{\parallel}}
of the
K3_{\parallel}
(
{\large{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}}_{\mathcal{C}_{2}^{\|}}\leq h_{K3_{\|}}^{1,1}
) as well as the 20 two-forms
J_{a_{\bot}}
of the
K3_{\bot}
(
{\large{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}}_{\mathcal{C}_{2}^{\bot}}\leq h_{K3_{\bot}}^{1,1}
). Below, we use the following shortened
representation

J={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{\parallel}\mathcal{J}_{\parallel}\right)+\frac{1}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{\perp}\mathcal{J}_{\perp}\right)

(110)

in order to avoid cumbersome expressions that are not necessary for our
analysis. More explicit calculations are obtained by replacing
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{\parallel}\mathcal{J}_{\parallel}
by the expansion
\sum{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{a_{\parallel}}\mathcal{J}_{a_{\parallel}}
and
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{\perp}\mathcal{J}_{\perp}
by
\sum{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{a_{\perp}}\mathcal{J}_{a_{\perp}}.
From this expansion of the Kahler 2-form on
K3_{\perp}\times K3_{\parallel}
, we notice a manifest symmetry property; it is invariant
under the discrete transformation

{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\leftrightarrow\frac{1}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}\qquad,\qquad{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{\parallel}\leftrightarrow{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{\perp}\qquad,\qquad\mathcal{J}_{\parallel}\leftrightarrow\mathcal{J}_{\perp}

(111)

Using the expansion (
110
) of the Kahler 2-form, we can compute the
monomial 2n-form
J^{n};
in particular volume 8-form
J^{4}
expanding as
follows

J^{4}

=

{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{4}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{\parallel}\right)^{4}\mathcal{J}_{\parallel}^{4}+4{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{\perp}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{\parallel}\right)^{3}\left(\mathcal{J}_{\parallel}^{3}\mathcal{J}_{\perp}\right)+

\frac{1}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{4}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{\perp}\right)^{4}\mathcal{J}_{\perp}^{4}+\frac{4}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{\parallel}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{\perp}\right)^{3}\mathcal{J}_{\parallel}\mathcal{J}_{\perp}^{3}+

6\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{\parallel}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{\perp}\right)^{2}\mathcal{J}_{\parallel}^{2}\mathcal{J}_{\perp}^{2}

(112)

This volume 8-form is also manifestly invariant under (
111
); just
because
J
does. Nevertheless, it is interesting to zoom on the action of
this symmetry transformation at the level of the five monomials
\mathcal{J}_{\parallel}^{n}\mathcal{J}_{\perp}^{4-n}
generating
J^{4}
. The two
first lines in (
112
) gets exchanged; while the third term
6\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{\parallel}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{\perp}\right)^{2}\mathcal{J}_{\parallel}^{2}\mathcal{J}_{\perp}^{2}
is exactly invariant; and is remarkably
independent of the parameter
\textstyle\mathchar 277
. So, a volume 8-form
J^{4}

independent of the parameter
\textstyle\mathchar 277
must be restricted to

J^{4}=6\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{\parallel}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{\perp}\right)^{2}\mathcal{J}_{\parallel}^{2}\mathcal{J}_{\perp}^{2}

(113)

and so should obey the constraint relations
\mathcal{J}_{\parallel}^{4}=\mathcal{J}_{\parallel}^{3}=0
but
\mathcal{J}_{\parallel}^{2}\neq 0;

otherwise the
J^{4}
diverges in the infinite distance limit
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow\infty.
By duality
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\leftrightarrow\left(1/{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\right),
it is the monomials
\mathcal{J}_{\perp}^{4}
and
\mathcal{J}_{\perp}^{3}
that should vanish in the ”short distance” limit
\left(1/{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\right)\rightarrow\infty.
So, we have

{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow\infty

:

\mathcal{J}_{\parallel}^{4}=\mathcal{J}_{\parallel}^{3}

=

0

\qquad,\qquad

\mathcal{J}_{\parallel}^{2}

\neq

0

\frac{1}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}\rightarrow\infty

:

\mathcal{J}_{\perp}^{4}=\mathcal{J}_{\perp}^{3}

=

0

\qquad,\qquad

\mathcal{J}_{\perp}^{2}

\neq

0

(114)

For the above specific Kahler 2-form (
112
), the volume
\mathcal{V}_{X_{4}}
of the CY4 is given by

\mathcal{V}_{CY_{4}}=\frac{1}{4!}\displaystyle\int\nolimits_{CY_{4}}J^{4}

which by using

{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}_{{}_{\|\|\bot\bot}}=\displaystyle\int\nolimits_{CY_{4}}\mathcal{J}_{\parallel}^{2}\wedge\mathcal{J}_{\perp}^{2}

gives

\mathcal{V}_{X_{4}}=\frac{1}{4}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}_{{}_{{\small\|\|\bot\bot}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{\parallel}\right)^{2}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{\perp}\right)^{2}

(117)

From these relations, we see that the total volume
\mathcal{V}_{X_{4}}
is
given by the product
\mathcal{V}_{K3_{\parallel}}\times\mathcal{V}_{K3_{\perp}}
with fiber
\mathcal{V}_{K3_{\perp}}
and base

volumes respectively given by
\frac{1}{2}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{\bot\bot}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{\perp}\right)^{2}
and
\frac{1}{2}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{{}_{\|\|}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{\parallel}\right)^{2}.
These expressions can be

CY_{4}=K3_{\perp}\times K3_{\parallel}
in (
116
); we find that
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}_{{}_{\|\|\bot\bot}}
factorises as the
product
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{{}_{\|\|}}\times{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{{}_{\bot\bot}}
with

{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{\|\|}=\displaystyle\int\nolimits_{K3_{\parallel}}\mathcal{J}_{\parallel}^{2}\qquad,\qquad{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{\bot\bot}=\displaystyle\int\nolimits_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}_{\bot}}\mathcal{J}_{\perp}^{2}

(118)

A graphic representation of the volumes of the two K3 surfaces

Graphic representation of the volumes of the fiber and the base of

As a result of this description, we cite the two following:

\mathbf{1)}

Given the fibration
X_{4}=S_{\bot}\times S_{\|},
the volumes of the fibral surface
S_{\bot}
and the base
S_{\|}

of the fibered Calabi-Yau fourfold scales in terms of the spectral parameter

\textstyle\mathchar 277
like

vol\left(S_{\bot}\right)=\frac{1}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}}\mathcal{V}_{K3_{\perp}}\quad,\quad vol\left(S_{\|}\right)={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}\mathcal{V}_{K3_{\parallel}}\quad,\quad vol\left(X_{4}\right)\simeq\mathcal{V}_{K3_{\parallel}}\times\mathcal{V}_{K3_{\perp}}

(119)

From this behavior, we see that the volumes
vol\left(S_{\bot}\right)

and
vol\left(S_{\|}\right)
take singular values in the
limits
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow\infty
and
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow 0
;
whilst the total volume of the Calabi-Yau fourfold
S_{\bot}\times S_{\|}
remains finite.

\mathbf{2)}

Moreover, for
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow\infty,
the
vol\left(S_{\bot}\right)
shrinks (
vol\left(S_{\bot}\right)\rightarrow 0
) while the
vol\left(S_{\|}\right)
diverges (
vol\left(S_{\|}\right)\rightarrow\infty
); this gives the
Asymptotic weak coupling [
\mathcal{G}_{\|\|}^{weak}\int_{M_{3D}}\left(F^{\|}\wedge\ast F^{\|}\right)
] with
gauge coupling metric

\mathcal{G}_{\|\|}^{weak}=\frac{1}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}}G_{\|\|}

(120)

with asymptotic behavior as
\mathcal{G}_{\|\|}^{weak}\sim\mathcal{O}\left(1/{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}\right)\rightarrow 0
. However, for
\left(1/{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\right)\rightarrow\infty,
we have the inverse picture; the
vol\left(S_{\|}\right)
shrinks but the
vol\left(S_{\bot}\right)
diverges.
The coupling [
\mathcal{G}_{\bot\bot}^{strong}\int_{M_{3D}}\left(F^{\bot}\wedge\ast F^{\bot}\right)
] has a ”strong coupling” metric

\mathcal{G}_{\bot\bot}^{strong}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}G_{\bot\bot}

(121)

with behavior at infinite distance
\mathcal{G}_{\bot\bot}^{strong}\sim\mathcal{O}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}\right)\rightarrow\infty.
Because the
change
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\leftrightarrow 1/{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}
is a symmetry of the Kahler
2-form (
112
) and the
EFT
3D
, the asymptotic weak
\mathcal{G}_{\parallel\parallel}^{weak}
and the short distance
\mathcal{G}_{\perp\perp}^{strong}
are dual in M-theory on
K3_{\perp}\times K3_{\parallel}
.

\mathbf{3)}

In the infinite distance limit (
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow\infty
), the fibral surface
S_{\bot}
shrinks; and there exists a non
singular curve
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\perp}}
in the
fiber with charge
q_{\text{{a}}}=(q_{i_{\bot}};q_{a_{\|}}=0)

where sits the weak gauge symmetry U
\left(1\right)_{weak}
. The complete
curve
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}
in the CY4 is then fully located in
K3_{\perp}.
By Weak/Strong gauge duality, we have a curve
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\parallel}}
and an abelian strong gauge symmetry U
\left(1\right)_{strong}
lying in the base
.

From these results, we conjecture that in the
EFT
3D

descending from M-theory on
K3_{\perp}\times K3_{\parallel}
with the
symmetry (
111
), we have two dual (weak and strong) asymptotic gauge
regimes; the
asymptotic strong gauge
regime is the image of the
asymptotic WGC under (
111
). Recall that asymptotic WGC stipulates that
weakly coupled directions in the fibral
K3_{\perp}
charge lattice host
towers of BPS or non-BPS states which satisfy the weak gravity conjecture. By using the weak/strong duality (
K3_{\perp}\leftrightarrow K3_{\parallel})
, we expect to also have towers of BPS or non-BPS states in the strong
gauge regime.

4.2
BPS and non BPS towers

As a consequence of the duality (
111
), the weakly coupled tower of BPS (
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{k}_{\mathrm{\bot}}\mathrm{,}\mathbf{q}_{\bot}}^{\text{{bps}}}
) or tower of non-BPS (
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{k}_{\mathrm{\bot}}\mathrm{,}\mathbf{q}_{\bot}}^{\text{{n-bps}}}
)
states in the
EFT
{}_{3D}.
These towers are predicted by the
Asymptotic WGC conjecture (
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow\infty
); they gets mapped
under (
111
) into strongly coupled towers of BPS (
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{k}_{\mathrm{\|}}\mathrm{,}\mathbf{q}_{\|}}^{\text{{bps}}}
) or
tower of non-BPS (
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{k}_{\mathrm{\|}}\mathrm{,}\mathbf{q}_{\|}}^{\text{{n-bps}}}
) states for the limit
\frac{1}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}\rightarrow\infty.
This correspondence between the two gauge
regimes (weak and strong) can be stated as follows

weak regime:
g_{weak}^{2}

\quad\leftrightarrow\quad

strong
regime:
g_{strong}^{2}

BPS
|
⊥

\leftrightarrow

BPS
|
∥

non-BPS
|
⊥

\leftrightarrow

non-BPS
|
∥

(122)

with
g_{weak}^{2}=\left(g_{{}_{\Upsilon}}^{2}\right)_{\perp\perp}
and
g_{strong}^{2}=\left(g_{{}_{\Upsilon}}^{2}\right)_{\parallel\parallel}
.
This result can be also motivated by the automorphism symmetry of
X_{4}=K3_{\parallel}\times K3_{\bot}
exchanging the fiber
K3_{\bot}

and the base
K3_{\parallel}.

From the Table (

) and borrowing results from

the

\left(i\right)

as

weak regime:
g_{weak}^{2}

\quad\leftrightarrow\quad

strong

g_{strong}^{2}

BPS

BPS

Recall that the

The second towers are given by

Because
