Having explored the weak/strong gauge duality in the
EFT
3D
, we turn now to investigate the asymptotic WGC in 3D. We
present the conjecture in addition to its viability constraints in claim I
and claim II; then we explicitly verify the conjecture for the different
towers of states.

5
The Asymptotic WGC and weak/strong duality

In this section, we give two claims (I and II) for Asymptotic WGC in the

EFT
3D
considered in this paper. These claims generalise
results obtained in five dimensional
EFT
5D
descending
from M-theory on Calabi-Yau threefolds. We also give new results for the
towers of BPS and non-BPS states in
EFT
3D
.

We start by noticing that since it was first proposed, the WGC has been
formulated in different ways and refined multiple times. The basic version
of the WGC in D-dimensions can be written as

m^{2}\leq\frac{D-2}{D-3}g^{2}q^{2}M_{\mathrm{Pl}}^{D-2}

(128)

Clearly this condition holds for
D>3;
however investigations of the
conjecture has been also performed in 3D; we cite for example
9A
;
9B
where the WGC has been studied from the context of infrared
consistency, and the condition of the WGC in 3D was expressed as

z^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2},\qquad z^{2}=\frac{q^{2}g^{2}}{m^{2}}M_{\mathrm{Pl}}

(129)

This constraints reads like

m^{2}\leq 2g^{2}q^{2}M_{Pl}

(130)

and is nothing but the (
128
) with the factor
(D-2)/(D-3)
replaced
by a factor of 2. Moreover, seen that 3D gravity is especially interesting
in the context of AdS
3
/CFT
2
, it is important to mention the study
conducted in
14G
where the WGC has been formulated as follows

\Delta=\tilde{\Delta}=\frac{\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}^{\prime}}{4}m^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\max\left(Q^{2},\tilde{Q}^{2}\right)

(131)

where

\Delta=L_{0}-\frac{c}{24},\qquad\tilde{\Delta}=\tilde{L}_{0}-\frac{c}{24}

(132)

with
Q,

\tilde{Q}
being the charges of the right and left movers
respectively. If we substitute
\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}^{\prime}
by 1/
M_{\mathrm{Pl}}
and
\max(Q^{2},\tilde{Q}^{2})
by
q^{2},
then the WGC conjecture
in 3D takes the form

m^{2}\leq 2g^{2}q^{2}M_{\mathrm{Pl}}

(133)

Given these facts, we come now to the 3D effective gauge theory coupled to
gravity with typical field action as given by eq(
41
). In this theory,
every direction
\Upsilon_{\mathrm{k}\mathbf{q}}
in the
even integral
charge lattice
\mathfrak{L}_{K3_{\parallel}\times K3_{\bot}}
, that is
dual to weakly coupled gauge group
U(1)_{weak}=U(1)_{\bot}
[sometimes
also denoted as
U(1)_{\Upsilon_{\mathrm{k}\mathbf{q}}}
], there exists a
tower
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{k,}\mathbf{q}}
of massive particle states
\left|\mathrm{k;}\mathbf{q;}M_{\mathrm{k}}\right\rangle
that satisfy
the inequality

M_{\mathrm{k}}^{2}\leq 2\mathrm{g}_{{\small 3D}}^{2}M_{\mathrm{Pl}}(q_{\text{{a}}}\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}q_{\text{{b}}})\mathrm{k}^{2}-\frac{1}{4}\mathfrak{g}^{\text{{xy}}}\frac{\partial M_{\mathrm{k}}}{\partial{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}}^{\text{{x}}}}\frac{\partial M_{\mathrm{k}}}{\partial{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}}^{\text{{y}}}}

(134)

In the inequality (
134
), the
\mathbf{q}=\left(q_{1},...,q_{r}\right)
is integral charge vector and the other parameters have the canonical
dimensions

\left[\mathrm{g}_{{\small 3D}}^{2}\right]=\left[M_{\mathrm{k}}\right]=mass^{1},\qquad\left[\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}\right]=\left[\mathfrak{g}^{\text{{xy}}}\right]=\left[{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}}^{\text{{x}}}\right]=mass^{0}

(135)

The
M_{\mathrm{k}}=M_{\mathrm{k}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}}\right)
is the mass of the
states in the BPS/non-BPS towers
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{k,}\mathbf{q}}^{\text{{bps}}}/\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{k,}\mathbf{q}}^{\text{{n-bps}}};

and the
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}}^{\text{{x}}}
s are the scalar fields present in the

EFT
3D
that are responsible for the Yukawa attractive force
\mathfrak{g}^{\text{{xy}}}(\partial_{\text{{x}}}M_{\mathrm{k}})(\partial_{\text{{y}}}M_{\mathrm{k}})
. Moreover, the
\mathfrak{g}^{\text{{xy}}}
is the inverse of the metric
\mathfrak{g}_{\text{{xy}}}
of the scalar manifold of the
EFT
3D
. In what
follows, we take all the volumes of the 2-cycles to be dimensionless in
order to make the calculations easier; this is done by using the normalised
variables
\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{a}}}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{\text{{a}}}/\mathcal{V}_{{\small CY}_{{\small 4}}}^{1/4}
satisfying the property

\frac{1}{4!}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}_{\text{{abcd}}}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{a}}}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{b}}}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{c}}}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{d}}}=\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{{\small CY}_{{\small 4}}}=1

(136)

To check the validity of the above inequality (
134
), we proceed in
steps as follows:

First, we need to express the scalar field metric
\mathfrak{g}^{\text{{xy}}}
in terms of the gauge coupling metric
\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}
used before. This is achieved by help of the following formula

\mathfrak{g}_{\text{{xy}}}d{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}}^{\text{{x}}}\wedge\ast d{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}}^{\text{{y}}}

=

\mathcal{G}_{\text{{ab}}}d\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{a}}}\wedge\ast d\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{b}}}

=

\left[\mathcal{G}_{\text{{ab}}}(\partial_{\text{{x}}}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{a}}})(\partial_{\text{{y}}}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{b}}})\right]d{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}}^{\text{{x}}}\wedge\ast d{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}}^{\text{{y}}}

(137)

from which we deduce the relationship
\mathfrak{g}_{\text{{xy}}}=\mathcal{G}_{\text{{ab}}}(\partial_{\text{{x}}}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{a}}})(\partial_{\text{{y}}}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{b}}}).
The inverse metric
\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}
is
related to the inverse
\mathfrak{g}^{\text{{xy}}}
as follows

\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}=\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{g}^{\text{{xy}}}(\partial_{\text{{x}}}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{a}}})(\partial_{\text{{y}}}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{b}}})+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 293$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 293$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 293$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 293$}}}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{a}}}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{b}}}

(138)

with
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 293$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 293$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 293$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 293$}}}=1/2.
Using these variables, the Asymptotic WGC condition (
134
) reads in terms of
\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}
then as
follows

M_{\mathrm{k}}^{2}\leq 2\mathrm{g}_{{\small 3D}}^{2}M_{\mathrm{Pl}}(q_{\text{{a}}}\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}q_{\text{{b}}})\mathrm{k}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}(\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 293$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 293$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 293$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 293$}}}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{a}}}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{b}}})\frac{\partial M_{\mathrm{k}}}{\partial\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{a}}}}\frac{\partial M_{\mathrm{k}}}{\partial\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{b}}}}

(139)

Since our discussion is focusing on the Calabi-Yau fourfold fibration
CY_{4}=K3_{\perp}\times K3_{\parallel},
we obtain a generalisation of the
claims given in
12
for the case of
EFT
5D

descending from the Calabi-Yau threefold fibration
CY_{3}=K3\times\mathbb{B}_{1}
.

5.1
Two claims: Weak/Strong gauge regimes

We give two claims (
I
and
II
); the claim-I concerns the
asymptotic weak gauge coupling in the WGC; the claim-
II
regards the
asymptotic strong gauge coupling in the WGC; it is the Weak/Strong gauge
dual of the claim-
I
.

5.1.1
Claim-I: Weakly coupled regime

In M-theory compactified on
K3_{\perp}\times K3_{\parallel}
, for every
direction in the associated even integral charge lattice
\mathfrak{L}=\mathfrak{L}_{\perp}\oplus\mathfrak{L}_{\parallel},
with a primitive
integral charge
\mathbf{q}=\mathbf{q}_{\perp}\oplus\mathbf{q}_{\parallel}
, we define two families of abelian gauge groups

U(1)_{\mathbf{q}_{\perp}}=\sum_{i_{\perp}=1}^{h_{\perp}^{1,1}}q_{i_{\perp}}U(1)^{i_{\perp}}\qquad,\qquad U(1)_{\mathbf{q}_{\parallel}}=\sum_{a_{\parallel}=1}^{h_{\parallel}^{1,1}}q_{a_{\parallel}}U(1)^{a_{\parallel}}

(140)

with the following properties:

(a)

Given the magnetic scale
\left(\Lambda_{{\small mag}}\right)_{U(1)_{\mathbf{q}_{\perp}}}
and the species scale
\left(\Lambda_{\text{{sp}}}\right)_{{}_{U(1)_{\mathbf{q}_{\perp}}}},

every direction
\mathbf{q}_{\perp}
in the fibral lattice
\mathfrak{L}_{\perp}
has a
U(1)_{\mathbf{q}_{\perp}}
gauge factor with weak
coupling limit in the infinite distance (
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow\infty)
in the sense

\left(\frac{\Lambda_{{\small mag}}}{\Lambda_{\text{{sp}}}}\right)_{U(1)_{\mathbf{q}_{\perp}}}\quad\rightarrow\quad 0,

(141)

admitting
\left(\mathbf{1}\right)
either a tower
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{k_{\perp},}\mathbf{q}_{\perp}}^{\text{{bps}}}=\mathcal{T}_{M_{\mathrm{k_{\perp}}}\rightarrow 0}^{\text{{bps}}}

o
f BPS
states; or
\left(\mathbf{2}\right)
a tower
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{k_{\perp},}\mathbf{q}_{\perp}}^{\text{{n-bps}}}=\mathcal{T}_{M_{\mathrm{k}_{{}_{\perp}}}\rightarrow 0}^{\text{{n-bps}}}
of non-BPS
states (
\mathrm{k_{\perp}}\in\mathbb{Z}
).

(b)

T
he BPS states, represented by the typical ket
\left|\mathrm{k_{\perp}};\mathbf{q_{\perp};}M_{\mathrm{k}_{\perp}}\right\rangle_{\text{{bps}}},
are given M2- brane wrapping
primitive 2-cycle
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\perp}}^{\text{{bps}}}
of
the fiber
K3_{\perp}
as illustrated by eqs(
123
-
124
). The
complex curve
\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{\mathbf{q}_{\perp}}^{\text{{bps}}}
(real 2-cycle associated with k
{}_{\perp}=1
) has positive self
intersection
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\perp}}^{\text{{bps}}}.\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\perp}}^{\text{{bps}}}=\mathbf{q}_{\perp}^{2}>0;
it
sits in the even integral self dual sublattice
\mathfrak{L}_{\perp}^{\left(+\right)}
of the fiber lattice
\mathfrak{L}_{\perp}
having
\mathbf{q}_{\perp}^{2}=2m
belonging to
2\mathbb{Z}.
Moreover, each state in the tower
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{k_{\perp},}\mathbf{q}_{\perp}}^{\text{{bps}}},
is characterised by the generic integer
\mathrm{k}_{\perp},
which is given by M2- brane wrapping 2-cycles
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\perp}}
lying in
\mathfrak{L}_{\perp}^{\left(+\right)}
multiple times.

(c)

The non-BPS states, represented by the typical ket
\left|n_{\mathbf{q}_{\perp}}^{L}\right\rangle_{\text{{n-bps}}},
are given by heterotic string excitations as in (
125
-
126
); this string follows from the usual M-theory/heterotic duality. This
heterotic string with left moving charge
n_{\mathbf{q}_{\perp}}^{L}
is
dual to the so-called Maldacena-Strominger-Witten (MSW) string given by M5
branes wrapping the generic fiber
K3_{\perp}.
Here, the
K3_{\perp}

contains a complex primitive curve
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\perp}}^{\text{{n-bps}}}
sitting in the anti-self dual sublattice
\mathfrak{L}_{\perp}^{\left(-\right)}
with
\left(\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}\right)

s
elf intersection
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\perp}}^{\text{{n-bps}}}.

\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\perp}}^{\text{{n-bps}}}=\mathbf{q}_{\perp}^{2}<0;
and
\left(\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}\right)
related to the string
excitation charge as
n_{\mathbf{q}_{\perp}}^{L}=-\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{q}_{\perp}^{2}.

Moreover, the tower
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{k_{\perp},}\mathbf{q}_{\perp}}^{\text{{n-bps}}}
is characterised by the generic integer
\mathrm{k}_{\perp};
as for the BPS case, the non BPS tower requires
K3_{\perp}

containing complex curve
\Upsilon_{\mathrm{k}_{\perp}\mathbf{q}_{\perp}}=

\mathrm{k}_{\perp}\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\perp}}^{\text{{n-bps}}}
sitting in the

sublattice
\mathfrak{L}_{\perp}^{\left(-\right)}
with self intersection
\mathbf{q}_{\perp}^{2}<0;

and string excitation modes as

n_{\mathrm{k}_{\perp}\mathbf{q}_{\perp}}^{L}=-\frac{\mathrm{k}_{\perp}^{2}}{2}\mathbf{q}_{\perp}^{2}

(142)

Given the claim-
I
and the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry (
111
),
we have the following dual claim-
II
assertion induced by
Weak/strong gauge duality.

5.1.2
Claim-II: Strongly coupled regime

Because of the duality symmetry of the
CY_{4}=K3_{\perp}\times K3_{\parallel}
acting by exchanging the two K3 surfaces (
K3_{\perp}\leftrightarrow K3_{\parallel}
), the properties given in Claim-I have a
dual homologue. So, to every direction in the sublattice
\mathfrak{L}_{\parallel},
dual to
\mathfrak{L}_{\perp},
in the even integral charge
lattice
\mathfrak{L}=\mathfrak{L}_{\perp}\oplus\mathfrak{L}_{\parallel}
; we have:

a)

a dual gauge symmetry factor
U(1)_{\mathbf{q}_{{}_{\parallel}}}
with vector charge
\mathbf{q}_{{}_{\parallel}}=(q_{1_{\parallel}},...,q_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}_{\parallel}})
with strong gauge
coupling limit in the sense,

\left(\frac{\Lambda_{{\small mag}}}{\Lambda_{\text{{sp}}}}\right)_{U(1)_{\mathbf{q}_{{}_{\parallel}}}}\quad\rightarrow\quad 0

(143)

admitting two towers of particle states in
EFT
{}_{{\small 3D}}:

\left(\mathbf{1}\right)
a tower
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{k}_{{}_{\parallel}},\mathbf{q}_{{}_{\parallel}}}^{\text{{bps}}}=\mathcal{T}_{M_{\mathrm{k_{\parallel}}}\rightarrow 0}^{\text{{bps}}}
of BPS particle
states; or
\left(\mathbf{2}\right)
a tower
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{k_{\parallel},}\mathbf{q}_{\parallel}}^{\text{{n-bps}}}=\mathcal{T}_{M_{\mathrm{k}_{{}_{\parallel}}}\rightarrow 0}^{\text{{n-bps}}}
of
non-BPS states. These two types of particles are described in the statements
(
b
) and (
c
) given below.

b)

t
he BPS states
|\mathrm{k_{{}_{\parallel}};}\mathbf{q_{{}_{\parallel}};}M_{\mathrm{k}_{{}_{\parallel}}}>_{\text{{bps}}}
are given M2 brane wrapping primitive 2-cycle
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{Q}_{{}_{\parallel}}}^{\text{{bps}}}
of the base
K3_{{}_{\parallel}}

with
\mathbf{Q}_{{}_{\parallel}}=\mathrm{k_{{}_{\parallel}}}\mathbf{q}_{{}_{\parallel}}.
T
he cycle
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{{}_{\parallel}}}^{\text{{bps}}}
(for the case k
{}_{{}_{\parallel}}=1
) has
positive self intersection with positive value
\mathbf{q}_{\parallel}^{2}>0
; it sits in the even integral (
\mathbf{q}_{\parallel}^{2}=2m_{\parallel}
)
s
elf dual sublattice
\mathfrak{L}_{\parallel}^{\left(+\right)}
of the base lattice
\mathfrak{L}_{\parallel}.

The tower
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{k}_{{}_{\parallel}},\mathbf{q}_{{}_{\parallel}}}^{\text{{bps}}}
of BPS particles is characterised by the integer
\mathrm{k}_{{}_{\parallel}};
it is generated by M2 brane wrapping the
multi-2-cycles
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{Q}_{{}_{\parallel}}}=

\mathrm{k}_{\parallel}\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\parallel}}^{\text{{bps}}}

expanding in the self dual sublattice
\mathfrak{L}_{\parallel}^{\left(+\right)}.

c)

The non-BPS states

|n_{\mathbf{q}_{\parallel}}^{L}>_{\text{{n-bps}}}
are given by the heterotic string
excitations. The heterotic string with left moving charge
n_{\mathbf{q}_{\parallel}}^{L}
is dual to the string given by M5 brane wrapping the
base surface
K3_{\parallel}.
Here, the
K3_{\parallel}
contains a
complex primitive curve
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\parallel}}^{\text{{n-bps}}}
sitting in the antiself dual sublattice
\mathfrak{L}_{\parallel}^{\left(-\right)}
with
\left(\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}\right)
negative self
intersection value
\mathbf{q}_{\perp}^{2}<0;
and
\left(\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}\right)
related to the string excitation charge as
n_{\mathbf{q}_{\parallel}}^{L}=-\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{q}_{\parallel}^{2}>0.

The tower
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{k}_{{}_{\parallel}},\mathbf{q}_{{}_{\parallel}}}^{\text{{n-bps}}}
is characterised by the generic integer
\mathrm{k}_{\parallel};
it requires
K3_{\parallel}
having a complex
curve
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{Q}_{\parallel}}=

k_{\parallel}\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\parallel}}^{\text{{n-bps}}}
sitting in the sublattice
\mathfrak{L}_{\perp}^{\left(-\right)}
with
\left(i\right)
negative
self intersection
\mathbf{q}_{\perp}^{2}<0;
and
\left(ii\right)
string excitation charge given by
n_{\mathrm{k}_{\parallel}\mathbf{q}_{\parallel}}^{L}=-\frac{\mathrm{k}_{\parallel}^{2}}{2}\mathbf{q}_{\parallel}^{2}.

The complex curves
\Upsilon_{\perp}
and
\Upsilon_{\parallel}
used
above depend on the structure of
K3_{\perp}\times K3_{\parallel},
that
is on whether they have degenerations at finite or infinite distance in the
moduli space of the
EFT
3D
. Recall that in the
EFT
5D

investigated in
12
; it was shown that in fibration of type
K3, the only curves that admit a weak coupling are of two types:
\left(\mathbf{i}\right)
K3 without degenerations; or
\left(\mathbf{ii}\right)

curves that degenerate in a finite distance in the moduli space. These
correspond to a generic K3 or degenerations classified as Kulikov type I, II
or III
27A
;
27B
. This result is general; and so hold also
for our
EFT
3D
studied in this paper. In our claims given above,
we mentioned that in directions of the charge lattice
\mathfrak{L}_{\perp}

(resp.
\mathfrak{L}_{\parallel}
) that allow for a weak (or the dual
strong) coupling limit, there exists towers of BPS or non-BPS states
satisfying the WGC (or its strong dual version).

Notice that the BPS bound in the
EFT
3D
align
with the extremality bound; and thus BPS states are super-extremal. This
feature was shown for
EFT
5D
in particular in
28
; there, the BPS states are all super-extremal, and
satisfy the WGC, not only in the infinite distance limit (
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow\infty
), but throughout the moduli space of the theory (
\textstyle\mathchar 277
finite); this is because charge to mass
ratio is protected by supersymmetry. However, non-BPS states do not have
this property.

In what follows, we test the conjecture for BPS and non-BPS states and weak/strong dual characterised by the conditions (
141
) and (
143
). We start by determining directions in the even integral charge lattice
\mathfrak{L}=\mathfrak{L}_{\perp}\oplus\mathfrak{L}_{\parallel}
of the CY4 that admit a weak coupling
limit. Then, we construct the towers
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{k},\mathbf{q}}^{\text{{bps}}}
and
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{k},\mathbf{q}}^{\text{{n-bps}}}
that host BPS and non-BPS states satisfying eqs(
141
-
143
).

5.2
Weakly/strongly coupled directions in the charge lattice

We start by focussing on the weak coupling limit at an infinite distance in
the moduli space (
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow\infty)
of the
EFT
3D
; and turn after to investigating the strongly coupled region dual to
asymptotic WGC. The asymptotic weak coupling limit is defined by the
constraint relation (
141
) namely
\left(\Lambda_{{\small mag}}/\Lambda_{\text{{sp}}}\right)_{U(1)_{\mathbf{q}}}\rightarrow 0

where
\Lambda_{{\small mag}}\equiv\Lambda_{\text{{wgc}}}
is the
magnetic WGC scale used earlier, and
\Lambda_{\text{{sp}}}\equiv\Lambda_{\text{{qg}}}
is the species scale below which quantum
gravity (QG) becomes weakly coupled. The reason that the weak gauge coupling
limit is defined in such a way is because when approaching the species scale

\Lambda_{\text{{sp}}}
, the dynamics of quantum gravity change.
Given a generic complex curve
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}=\sum_{\text{{a}}=1}^{h_{{\small X}_{{\small 4}}}^{{\small 1,1}}}q_{\text{{a}}}\mathcal{C}^{\text{{a}}}
in the Calabi-Yau fourfold
K3_{\perp}\times K3_{\parallel},
the magnetic WGC scale in terms of the
3D Planck mass
M_{\mathrm{Pl}}
and the Yang Mills gauge coupling
\mathrm{g}_{{\small 3D}}
is defined as

\left.\Lambda_{{\small mag}}^{2}\right|_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}}=\mathrm{g}_{{\small 3D}}^{2}M_{\mathrm{Pl}}(q_{\text{{a}}}\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}q_{\text{{b}}})

(144)

Using the expression of the gauge coupling metric
\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}
given by eq(
85
), this magnetic scale expands as follows

\displaystyle\left.\Lambda_{{\small mag}}^{2}\right|_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}\mathrm{g}_{{\small 3D}}^{2}M_{\mathrm{Pl}}(q_{a_{\parallel}}\mathring{G}^{a_{\parallel}b_{\parallel}}q_{b_{\parallel}})+

(145)

\displaystyle 2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{0}\mathrm{g}_{{\small 3D}}^{2}M_{\mathrm{Pl}}(q_{a_{\parallel}}\mathring{G}^{a_{\parallel}i_{{}_{\perp}}}q_{i_{{}_{\perp}}})+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}}\mathrm{g}_{{\small 3D}}^{2}M_{\mathrm{Pl}}(q_{i_{\perp}}\mathring{G}^{i_{\perp}j_{\perp}}q_{j_{\perp}})

It has three block terms captured by diagonal coupling sub-matrices
\mathcal{G}^{i_{\perp}j_{\perp}}
and
\mathcal{G}^{a_{\parallel}b_{\parallel}}
as well as the cross block
\mathcal{G}^{a_{\parallel}i_{{}_{\perp}}}.
From this expansion, we learn several features; in
particular the two interesting following:

\bullet

Weakly gauge coupled directions

For fixed values of the scale
\Lambda_{\text{{sp}}},
the weakly
coupled directions in
K3_{\perp}\times K3_{\parallel}
that realises the
asymptotic weak gauge coupling condition
\left(\Lambda_{{\small mag}}/\Lambda_{\text{{sp}}}\right)_{U(1)_{\mathbf{q}}}\rightarrow 0
is
given by the
necessary
condition
\left.\Lambda_{{\small mag}}^{2}\right|_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}}\rightarrow 0.
This necessary
condition is solved in eq(
145
) by requiring

q_{a_{\parallel}}=0

(146)

thus restricting the complex curve
\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{\mathbf{q}}
to sit
completely in the fiber
K3_{\perp}
of the CY
4
; that is restricting
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}
to the fibral curve
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\perp}}

with expansion as
\sum_{i_{\perp}=1}^{h_{K3_{\perp}}^{{\small 1,1}}}q_{i_{\perp}}\mathcal{C}^{i_{\perp}}.
With this restriction, eqs(
144
-
145
) read as follows

\left.\Lambda_{{\small mag}}^{2}\right|_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\perp}}}=\mathrm{g}_{{\small 3D}}^{2}(q_{i_{\perp}}\mathcal{G}^{i_{\perp}j_{\perp}}q_{j_{\perp}})=\frac{1}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}}\mathrm{g}_{{\small 3D}}^{2}(q_{i_{\perp}}\mathring{G}^{i_{\perp}j_{\perp}}q_{j_{\perp}})

(147)

By setting
\mathrm{\mathring{g}}_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\perp}}}^{2}=\mathrm{g}_{{\small 3D}}^{2}(q_{i_{\perp}}\mathring{G}^{i_{\perp}j_{\perp}}q_{j_{\perp}})
with no dependence in the scaling parameter
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}},

the above relation can be presented simply as

\left.\Lambda_{{\small mag}}^{2}\right|_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\perp}}}=\frac{1}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}}\mathrm{\mathring{g}}_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\perp}}}^{2}

(148)

So, the magnetic scale behaves in the infinite distance limit (
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow\infty)
like

\left.\Lambda_{{\small mag}}^{2}\right|_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\perp}}}\sim\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}}\right)\rightarrow 0

(149)

and then
\left(\Lambda_{{\small mag}}/\Lambda_{\text{{sp}}}\right)_{U(1)_{\mathbf{q}_{\perp}}}\rightarrow 0
.

\bullet

Strongly gauge coupled directions

For fixed values of
\Lambda_{\text{{sp}}},
the strongly coupled
directions in
K3_{\perp}\times K3_{\parallel},
that are dual to the weak
ones, realises the asymptotic strong coupling condition (
), the inverse of (
141
). This condition
\left(\Lambda_{{\small mag}}/\Lambda_{\text{{sp}}}\right)_{U(1)_{\mathbf{q}}}\rightarrow\infty
can be realised by the
necessary
condition
\left.\Lambda_{mag}^{2}\right|_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}}\rightarrow\infty.
From eq(
144
), we see that this necessary condition is
naturally solved by demanding

q_{i_{\perp}}=0

(150)

thus restricting the complex curve
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}
to completely
sitting in the base
K3_{\parallel}
of the CY
4
; that is restricting
\mathcal{\Upsilon}_{\mathbf{q}}
to base curves
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{{}_{\parallel}}}
given by
\sum_{a_{\parallel}=1}^{h_{K3\parallel}^{{\small 1,1}}}q_{a_{\parallel}}\mathcal{C}^{a_{\parallel}}.
So the
magnetic scale (
144
) reads as

\left.\Lambda_{{\small mag}}^{2}\right|_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\parallel}}}=\mathrm{g}_{{\small 3D}}^{2}M_{\mathrm{Pl}}(q_{a_{\parallel}}\mathcal{G}^{a_{\parallel}b_{\parallel}}q_{b_{\parallel}})={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}\mathrm{g}_{{\small 3D}}^{2}M_{\mathrm{Pl}}(q_{a_{\parallel}}\mathring{G}^{a_{\parallel}b_{\parallel}}q_{b_{\parallel}})

(151)

By setting
\mathrm{\mathring{g}}_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{n}_{\parallel}}}^{2}=\mathrm{g}_{{\small 3D}}^{2}(q_{a_{\parallel}}\mathring{G}^{a_{\parallel}b_{\parallel}}q_{b_{\parallel}})
with finite dependence in the large
limit of
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}},
this relation can be presented just like

\left.\Lambda_{{\small mag}}^{2}\right|_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\parallel}}}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}\mathrm{\mathring{g}}_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{n}_{\parallel}}}^{2}

(152)

behaving like
\left.\Lambda_{{\small mag}}^{2}\right|_{\mathcal{\Upsilon}_{\mathbf{n}_{\parallel}}}\sim\mathcal{O}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}\right)
and
\left.\Lambda_{{\small mag}}^{2}\right|_{\mathcal{\Upsilon}_{\mathbf{n}_{\parallel}}}\rightarrow\infty.
Therefore, for
fixed
\Lambda_{\text{{sp}}},
we have
\left(\Lambda_{{\small mag}}/\Lambda_{\text{{sp}}}\right)_{U(1)_{\mathbf{q}_{\parallel}}}\rightarrow\infty.

\bullet

About the species scale

\Lambda_{SP}

First, recall that the species scale
\Lambda_{SP}
is defined as the scale

beyond
which quantum gravity becomes strongly coupled. A general
formula defining the species scale in D-dimensional space time is
given by
3A
;
29

\Lambda_{\text{{sp}}}=\frac{M_{\mathrm{Pl}}^{{\tiny[D]}}}{N^{1/\left(D-2\right)}}

(153)

where
M_{\mathrm{Pl}}^{{\tiny[D]}}
is the D-dimensional Planck mass, and
N
is the number of species. In our
EFT
3D
descending from
M-theory on
K3_{\perp}
-fibered Calabi-Yau fourfold on
K3_{\|}
, the
species scale
\Lambda_{\text{{sp}}}
and the heterotic scale
\mathrm{M}_{het}
are linked by a logarithmic behaviour via

\Lambda_{\text{{sp}}}^{2}\sim\mathrm{M}_{{\small het}}^{2}\log(\frac{\mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{Pl}}}{\mathrm{M}_{{\small het}}})>0\qquad,\qquad\mathrm{M}_{{\small het}}<\mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{Pl}}

(154)

On the other hand taking the K3 volume
\mathcal{V}_{K3}
to be
dimensionless, the heterotic string tension
T_{het}=2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\mathrm{M}_{het}^{2}
is expressed in terms of the K3 volume as
T_{het}=2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\mathcal{V}_{K3}\mathrm{M}_{11d}^{2}
and then
\mathrm{M}_{{\small het}}^{2}=\mathrm{M}_{11d}^{2}\mathcal{V}_{K3}.
So, we distinguish

\mathrm{M}_{{\small het}_{\perp}}^{2}=\mathrm{M}_{11d}^{2}\mathcal{V}_{X_{\perp}}\qquad,\qquad\mathrm{M}_{{\small het}_{\|}}^{2}=\mathrm{M}_{11d}^{2}\mathcal{V}_{K3_{{}_{\|}}}

(155)

Moreover, because under infinite distance scaling (
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow\infty
), the volume
\mathcal{V}_{K3_{\perp}}
of the fiber
K3_{\perp}

scales like
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{-2}\mathcal{V}_{K3_{\perp}}
while the volume
\mathcal{V}_{K3_{\|}}
of the base
K3_{{}_{\|}}
scales like
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}\mathcal{V}_{K3_{{}_{\|}}},
the scaling of the heterotic mass scales
\mathrm{M}_{het\bot}
and
\mathrm{M}_{het\|}
respectively on the
fiber and on the base of the CY4 behave as follows

\mathrm{M}_{het\bot}\sim\frac{1}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}\mathrm{M}_{11d}\qquad,\qquad\mathrm{M}_{het\|}\sim{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\mathrm{M}_{11d}

(156)

As such, in the infinite distance limit, we have
\mathrm{M}_{het\bot}\rightarrow 0
while
\mathrm{M}_{het\|}\rightarrow\infty.
By
substituting into (
154
), the species scale
\Lambda_{\text{{sp}}_{\bot}}
behaves for the asymptotic weak gauge coupling as

\Lambda_{\text{{sp}}_{\bot}}^{2}\sim\frac{\mathrm{M}_{11d}^{2}}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}}\left[\log\frac{\mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{Pl}}}{\mathrm{M}_{11d}}+\log{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\right]

(157)

For the strongly coupled regions the species scale
\Lambda_{\text{{sp}}_{\|}}
behaves like:

\Lambda_{\text{{sp}}_{\|}}^{2}\sim{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}\mathrm{M}_{11d}^{2}\log\frac{\mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{Pl}}}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\mathrm{M}_{11d}}

(158)

indicating that we must have
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\mathrm{M}_{11d}\leq\mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{Pl}}
and then
\textstyle\mathchar 277
is bounded as
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\leq\mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{Pl}}/\mathrm{M}_{11d}.
Consequently, the asymptotic weak gauge
coupling condition reading like

\frac{(\Lambda_{{\small mag}}^{2})_{{}_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\perp}}}}}{\Lambda_{\text{{sp}}_{\bot}}^{2}}=\frac{\mathrm{\mathring{g}}_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\perp}}}^{2}}{\mathrm{M}_{11d}\log{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}

(159)

tends indeed to zero in the infinite distance limit
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow\infty;
thus showing the validity of the weak coupling limit condition (
73
-
141
). In this regards, it was shown in
12
that in fibration of type K3 of the CY3 of the
EFT
5D
, the curves
\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{q}}
that
admit a weak gauge coupling symmetry
U\left(1\right)_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{q}}}
are either in a non degenerate fiber K3, or the
ones that degenerate in a finite distance in the moduli space. This result
is general since it only depends on the geometry of
K3
; and consequently
holds also for the curves
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\perp}}
used in our
EFT
{}_{3D}\mathrm{.}

As for the strongly coupled region, which is dual to the asymptotic weakly
coupled region, the check of the condition is given by
\left(\Lambda_{{\small mag}}/\Lambda_{\text{{sp}}}\right)_{U(1)_{\mathbf{q}_{\parallel}}}\rightarrow\infty
when the scaling parameter
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow\mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{Pl}}/\mathrm{M}_{11d}.
By substituting
(\Lambda_{{\small mag}}^{2})_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\parallel}}}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}\mathrm{\mathring{g}}_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\parallel}}}^{2}
and
\Lambda_{\text{{sp}}_{\|}}
by its expression (
158
), the
condition reads as follows

\frac{(\Lambda_{{\small mag}}^{2})_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\parallel}}}}{\Lambda_{\text{{sp}}_{\|}}^{2}}=\frac{\mathrm{\mathring{g}}_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\parallel}}}^{2}}{\mathrm{M}_{11d}^{2}\log\frac{\mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{Pl}}}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\mathrm{M}_{11d}}}

(160)

it tends to infinity for
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow\mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{Pl}}/\mathrm{M}_{11d}
in agreement with the weak/strong duality property of the

EFT
3D
considered in this study.

In what follows we investigate the towers of states along these weak and
strong directions in the charge lattice in
K3_{\|}\times K3_{\bot}.

We start with the tower
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{k},\mathbf{q}}^{\text{{bps}}}
BPS states in the

3D
, and then move on to the tower

of non-BPS

5.2.1
Towers of BPS states and asymptotic gauge couplings

Having determined the directions in the even integral charge
lattice
\mathfrak{L}=\mathfrak{L}_{\perp}\oplus\mathfrak{L}_{\parallel}

in the CY4 that are weakly coupled in
K3_{\bot}
(and strongly in
K3_{\|}
due to weak/gravity duality), we turn now to explicitly verify
if the states in these directions satisfy the tower WGC and
its
strong dual version
. To that purpose, recall that the WGC tower of states
satisfies the relation (
141
); these states include BPS states
occupying rays in the self dual sublattice
\mathfrak{L}_{\perp}^{\left(+\right)}
; and the non BPS states living in the anti-self dual
\mathfrak{L}_{\perp}^{\left(-\right)}.

For the case of the BPS tower
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{k}_{\bot},\mathbf{q}_{\bot}}^{\text{{bps}}}
, it is built out of M2 brane wrapping
complex curve
\Upsilon_{\mathrm{k}\mathbf{q}_{\bot}}
in
\mathfrak{L}_{\perp}^{\left(+\right)}
having positive self intersection (
\Upsilon_{\mathrm{k}_{\bot}\mathbf{q}_{\bot}}^{2}>0

this feature extends naturally to a dual BPS tower

sitting in
\mathfrak{L}_{\|}^{\left(+\right)}
. For these two BPS towers, the
masses

of their particle states is given by

M_{\mathrm{k}}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 280$}}}M_{11d}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{\text{{a}}=1}^{h_{{\small CY4}}^{1,1}}\left(\mathrm{k}q_{\text{{a}}}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{a}}}\right)\qquad,\qquad M_{11d}=\frac{M_{\mathrm{Pl}}}{\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}}\mathcal{V}_{{\small CY}_{{\small 4}}}}

(161)

with integers k
x
and dimensionless volume

and
\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}=4{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}
and
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 280$}}}=1/\sqrt{2}.
From this
expression of the mass, which splits like the sum of

with

M_{\mathrm{k}_{\bot}}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 280$}}}M_{11d}\sum_{i_{\bot}=1}^{h_{\bot}^{1,1}}\left(\mathrm{k}_{\bot}q_{i_{\bot}}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{i_{\bot}}\right)\qquad,\qquad M_{\mathrm{k}_{\|}}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 280$}}}M_{11d}\sum_{a_{\|}=1}^{h_{\|}^{1,1}}\left(\mathrm{k}_{\|}q_{a_{\|}}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{a_{\|}}\right)

(162)

we can check the asymptotic WGC condition (

) and its strong regime

141
).

Now, given eq(

), we learn that
M_{\mathrm{k}}^{2}
is equal to
4{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}^{2}\mathrm{k}^{2}\left(q_{\text{{a}}}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{a}}}\right)^{2}M_{11d}^{2}
, it is quadratic in the volumes

and in

as well as the integral

; it splits into three blocks as follows

(163)

where the fibral

and the base

are quadratic in (

respectively.

A. Repulsive Force Conjecture (RFC):

Given a massive BPS particle in the
EFT
3D
of mass
M_{\mathrm{k}}
, the repulsive force conjecture (RFC), which in the infinite
distance limit coincides with the asymptotic WGC, requires the following
inequality
3A
;
6A
;
6B

F_{Coulomb}\geq F_{Grav}+F_{Yukawa}

(164)

This inequality reads explicitly as follows

\frac{\mathrm{g}_{{\small 3D}}^{2}}{M_{\mathrm{Pl}}}\left(\mathrm{k}^{2}q_{\text{{a}}}\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}q_{\text{{b}}}\right)\geq\left.\frac{D-3}{D-2}\right|_{D=3}\frac{M_{\mathrm{k}}^{2}}{M_{\mathrm{Pl}}^{2}}+\mathcal{Y}

(165)

with Yukawa matter coupling contribution
\mathcal{Y}
reading in terms of
the reduced mass
M_{\mathrm{k}}
as follows

\displaystyle M_{\mathrm{Pl}}^{2}\mathcal{Y}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}}{2M_{\mathrm{k}}^{2}}\left(\frac{\partial M_{\mathrm{k}}^{2}}{\partial\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{a}}}}\right)\left(\frac{\partial M_{\mathrm{k}}^{2}}{\partial\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{b}}}}\right)-\frac{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 293$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 293$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 293$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 293$}}}}{M_{\mathrm{k}}^{2}}\left[\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{a}}}\frac{\partial M_{\mathrm{k}}^{2}}{\partial\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{a}}}}\right]^{2}

(166)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}\left(\frac{\partial M_{\mathrm{k}}}{\partial\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{a}}}}\right)\left(\frac{\partial M_{\mathrm{k}}}{\partial\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{b}}}}\right)-4{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 293$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 293$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 293$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 293$}}}\left[\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{a}}}\frac{\partial M_{\mathrm{k}}}{\partial\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{a}}}}\right]^{2}

(167)

with
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 293$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 293$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 293$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 293$}}}=1/2.
Notice that because here
D=3,
the contribution from
classical gravity namely
\left(D-3\right)/\left(D-2\right)
vanishes
identically; this is expected because massless 3D gravity is topological.
However, a non trivial contribution may come from quantum effect; we denote
it like
\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}}}_{{\small QG}}
. Substituting, the RFC
inequality (
165
) becomes

\mathrm{g}_{{\small 3D}}^{2}M_{\mathrm{Pl}}\left(\mathrm{k}^{2}q_{\text{{a}}}\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}q_{\text{{b}}}\right)\geq\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}}}_{{\small QG}}+\frac{\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}}{2M_{\mathrm{k}}^{2}}\left(\frac{\partial M_{\mathrm{k}}^{2}}{\partial\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{a}}}}\right)\left(\frac{\partial M_{\mathrm{k}}^{2}}{\partial\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{b}}}}\right)-\frac{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 293$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 293$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 293$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 293$}}}}{M_{\mathrm{k}}^{2}}\left[\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{a}}}\frac{\partial M_{\mathrm{k}}^{2}}{\partial\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{a}}}}\right]^{2}

(168)

with charge
q_{\text{{a}}}=\left(q_{i_{\perp}},q_{a_{\|}}\right).
We rewrite this inequality by putting the mass contribution to
the right hand side as follows

\frac{\mathrm{g}_{{\small 3D}}^{2}M_{\mathrm{Pl}}}{4}\left(\mathrm{k}^{2}q_{\text{{a}}}\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}q_{\text{{b}}}\right)\geq\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}}}_{{\small QG}}+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}\left(\frac{\partial M_{\mathrm{k}_{x}}}{\partial\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{a}}}}\right)\left(\frac{\partial M_{\mathrm{k}_{x}}}{\partial\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{b}}}}\right)-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 293$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 293$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 293$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 293$}}}\left[\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{a}}}\frac{\partial M_{\mathrm{k}_{x}}}{\partial\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{a}}}}\right]^{2}

(169)

Because the metric inverse
\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}
plays an
important role in this relation and its test, we first give details on its
calculation; and turn after to verify the validity of the repulsive force
conjecture for the tower of BPS states. Below, we assume
\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}}}_{{\small QG}}<0
for BPS particle states in vacuum
TPA
;
TPB
; so to test (
168
-
169
), we can disregard
\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}}}_{{\small QG}}
without affection

B. Deriving the inverse of gauge metric (
221
):

In eq(
204
), the matrix
\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}
is the
inverse of the gauge coupling metric
\mathcal{G}_{\text{{ab}}}
;
this is a
h_{{\small CY4}}^{1,1}\times h_{{\small CY4}}^{1,1}
matrix
decomposing into 4 bloc submatrices as follows,

\mathcal{G}_{\text{{ab}}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathcal{G}_{a_{\|}b_{\|}}&\mathcal{G}_{a_{\|}j_{\perp}}\\
\mathcal{G}_{i_{\perp}b_{\|}}&\mathcal{G}_{i_{\perp}j_{\perp}}\end{array}\right),\qquad\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathcal{G}^{a_{\|}b_{\|}}&\mathcal{G}^{a_{\|}j_{\perp}}\\
\mathcal{G}^{i_{\perp}b_{\|}}&\mathcal{G}^{i_{\perp}j_{\perp}}\end{array}\right)

(170)

The sub-bloc
\mathcal{G}_{a_{\|}b_{\|}}
is a square matrix
h_{\|}^{1,1}\times h_{\|}^{1,1},
the sub-bloc
\mathcal{G}_{i_{\perp}j_{\perp}}
is also a square matrix
h_{\perp}^{1,1}\times h_{\perp}^{1,1}.
The sub-bloc
\mathcal{G}_{a_{\|}j_{\perp}}
and
\mathcal{G}_{i_{\perp}b_{\|}}
are matrices
h_{\|}^{1,1}\times h_{\perp}^{1,1}
and
h_{\perp}^{1,1}\times h_{\|}^{1,1}.
For the
case
h_{\|}^{1,1}=h_{\perp}^{1,1}
required by Weak/Strong duality,
these blocs are square matrices. Below, we use le large (
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow\infty
) and short (
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow 0
) distance limits to
compute the metric components in eq(
170
).

similarly for the other quantities like

T_{\mathrm{s}_{x}}
and
\Delta_{{\small CB}_{x}}.
This is to say that for the
K3_{\bot}
fiber, we have a fibral tower
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{n}_{\bot}}^{\text{{n-bps}}}
of particles with masse
M_{\mathrm{n}_{\bot}}^{2};

and for the base surface
K3_{\|}
we have a base tower
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{n}_{\parallel}}^{\text{{n-bps}}}
of particles with
M_{\mathrm{n}_{\|}}^{2}.
In sum, depending on whether M5 wraps
K3_{\bot}
or
K3_{\|},
we distinguish two kinds of towers of states with
squared masses as follows

M_{\mathrm{n}_{\bot}}^{2}

=

8{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\mathrm{n}_{{}_{\bot}}T_{\mathrm{s}_{\bot}}+\Delta_{{\small CB}_{\bot}}

M_{\mathrm{n}_{\|}}^{2}

=

8{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\mathrm{n}_{\|}T_{\mathrm{s}\|}+\Delta_{{\small CB}_{\|}}

(200)

and values at infinite distance limit like

\lim_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow\infty}\left(M_{\mathrm{n}_{\bot}}^{2}\right)=0\qquad,\qquad\lim_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow\infty}\left(M_{\mathrm{n}_{\|}}^{2}\right)=\infty

(201)

These towers of non BPS states fill directions
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{x}}

in the antiself dual
\mathfrak{L}_{-}^{\left(x\right)};
the latter is a
charge sublattice contained in the even integral lattice
\mathfrak{L}^{\left(x\right)}
of K3
x
. Recall that for the Calabi-Yau fourfold
K3_{\bot}\times K3_{\|}
, the charge lattice is given by
\mathfrak{L}^{\left(\bot\right)}\oplus\mathfrak{L}^{\left(\|\right)}
with
\mathfrak{L}^{\left(x\right)}
splitting into self dual and antiself dual
parts as
\mathfrak{L}^{\left(x\right)}=\mathfrak{L}_{+}^{\left(x\right)}\oplus\mathfrak{L}_{-}^{\left(x\right)}
with label
x=\bot,\|.

Recall also that for non BPS states, the basic curve
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{x}}
has negative self intersection (
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{x}}^{2}=\mathbf{q}_{x}^{2}<0
); as such these non BPS states are labeled by the
positive integer
\mathrm{n}_{x}
giving the left moving excitation mode of
the heterotic string. Moreover, the
\Delta_{CB_{x}}
in (
199
) is

\Delta_{CB_{x}}=\digamma\left(\sum_{i_{x}=1}^{h_{x}^{1,1}}q_{i_{x}}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{i_{x}}\right)^{2}\qquad,\qquad\digamma=4{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}^{2}\mathrm{M}_{11d}^{2}

(202)

This
\Delta_{CB_{x}}
is the contribution from the Coulomb
branch just as in the
EFT
5D

12
; it is
quadratic in the volumes
\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{i_{x}}
of the 2-cycles generating
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}=\sum q_{i_{x}}\mathcal{C}^{i_{x}}.

Furthermore, the tension
T_{\mathrm{s}_{\bot}}
(resp.
T_{\mathrm{s}_{\|}}
) of the string is given in terms of the volume
\mathcal{\hat{V}}_{K3_{\bot}}
(resp.
\mathcal{\hat{V}}_{K3_{\parallel}}
) as follows

T_{\mathrm{s}_{x}}=2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\mathcal{\hat{V}}_{K3_{x}}\mathrm{M}_{11d}^{2}\qquad,\text{\qquad}\mathrm{M}_{11d}=\frac{M_{\mathrm{Pl}}}{\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}}\mathcal{V}_{{\small CY}_{{\small 4}}}}

(203)

with
\mathcal{\hat{V}}_{{\small K3}}
given by
\mathcal{\hat{V}}_{{\small K3}_{{\small\bot}}}=(\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{i_{\perp}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{i_{\perp}j_{\perp}}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{j_{\perp}})/2
for the fiber with scaling in the
infinite distance limit as
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{-2}\mathcal{\hat{V}}_{{\small K3}_{{\small\bot}}}
; and
\mathcal{\hat{V}}_{{\small K3}_{\|}}=(\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{a_{\|}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{a_{\|}b_{\|}}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{b_{\|}})/2
for the base with scaling
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{+2}\mathcal{\hat{V}}_{{\small K3}_{\|}}
. Substituting these values, we have

M_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}

=

{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 292$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 292$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 292$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 292$}}}_{\mathrm{n}_{{}_{x}}}\left[\mathcal{\hat{V}}_{K3_{x}}+\frac{1}{4\mathrm{n}_{{}_{x}}}\left(q_{i_{x}}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{i_{x}}\right)^{2}\right]

\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}

=

{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}_{\mathrm{n}_{{}_{x}}}\left[\mathcal{\hat{V}}_{K3_{x}}+\frac{1}{4\mathrm{n}_{{}_{x}}}\left(q_{i_{x}}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{i_{x}}\right)^{2}\right]

(204)

where we have set
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 292$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 292$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 292$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 292$}}}_{\mathrm{n}_{{}_{x}}}=16{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}^{2}\mathrm{M}_{11d}^{2}\mathrm{n}_{{}_{x}};
and for later use we have introduced the reduced squared
mass
\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}=M_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}/M_{\mathrm{Pl}}^{2}
with
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 292$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 292$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 292$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 292$}}}_{\mathrm{n}_{{}_{x}}}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 292$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 292$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 292$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 292$}}}_{\mathrm{n}_{{}_{x}}}/M_{\mathrm{Pl}}^{2}.
By using
M_{\mathrm{Pl}}^{2}=16{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}^{2}\mathcal{V}_{{\small CY}_{{\small 4}}}^{2}\mathrm{M}_{11d}^{2},
the
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}_{\mathrm{n}_{{}_{x}}}
reads also as follows

{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}_{\mathrm{n}_{{}_{x}}}=\frac{16{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}^{2}\mathrm{M}_{11d}^{2}}{M_{\mathrm{Pl}}^{2}}\mathrm{n}_{{}_{x}}=\frac{1}{\mathcal{V}_{{\small X}_{{\small 4}}}^{2}}\mathrm{n}_{{}_{x}}

(205)

For later use, notice also that
M_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}
and
\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}
are homogeneous functions in the 2-cycle volumes
\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{i_{x}}
; as such they are eigenstates of the operator
L_{0}=\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{i_{x}}(\partial/\partial\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{i_{x}})
in the sense

L_{0}M_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}=2M_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}\qquad,\qquad L_{0}\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}=2\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}
