Notice moreover that being quadratic in the moduli
\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{i_{x}},

the
\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}
can be also nicely presented like

\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}=\frac{1}{2}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{i_{x}}\mathcal{S}_{i_{x}j_{x}}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{j_{x}}

(207)

with symmetric matrix
\mathcal{S}_{i_{x}j_{x}}
given by

\mathcal{S}_{i_{x}j_{x}}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}_{\mathrm{n}_{{}_{x}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{i_{x}j_{x}}+\frac{1}{2\mathrm{n}_{{}_{x}}}q_{i_{x}}q_{j_{x}}\right)

(208)

From this relation, we compute several interesting quantities; in particular

\frac{\partial\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}}{\partial\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{i_{x}}}=\mathcal{S}_{i_{x}k_{x}}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{k_{x}},\qquad\frac{\partial\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}}{\partial\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{j_{x}}}=\mathcal{S}_{j_{x}l_{x}}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{l_{x}}

(209)

and

{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}^{k_{x}l_{x}}\left(\frac{\partial\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}}{\partial\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{i_{x}}}\right)\left(\frac{\partial\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}}{\partial\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{j_{x}}}\right)=\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{k_{x}}\Delta_{i_{x}j_{x}}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{l_{x}}

(210)

where we have set
\Delta_{i_{x}j_{x}}=\mathcal{S}_{i_{x}k_{x}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}^{k_{x}l_{x}}\mathcal{S}_{l_{x}j_{x}}
reading explicitly as follows

\Delta_{i_{x}j_{x}}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}_{\mathrm{n}_{{}_{x}}}^{2}\left[{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{i_{x}j_{x}}+\frac{1}{\mathrm{n}_{{}_{x}}}\left(1+\frac{\mathbf{q}_{x}^{2}}{4\mathrm{n}_{{}_{x}}}\right)q_{i_{x}}q_{j_{x}}\right]

(211)

By using the relation
\mathbf{q}_{x}^{2}=-2\mathrm{n}_{{}_{x}},
it
simplifies like

\Delta_{i_{x}j_{x}}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}_{\mathrm{n}_{{}_{x}}}^{2}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{i_{x}j_{x}}+\frac{1}{2\mathrm{n}_{{}_{x}}}q_{i_{x}}q_{j_{x}}\right)

(212)

By comparing this relation with the matrix
\mathcal{S}_{i_{x}j_{x}}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}_{\mathrm{n}_{{}_{x}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{i_{x}j_{x}}+\frac{1}{2\mathrm{n}_{{}_{x}}}q_{i_{x}}q_{j_{x}}\right),
we learn that we just have

\Delta_{i_{x}j_{x}}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}_{\mathrm{n}_{{}_{x}}}\mathcal{S}_{i_{x}j_{x}}

(213)

So, we have

{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}^{k_{x}l_{x}}\left(\frac{\partial\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}}{\partial\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{i_{x}}}\right)\left(\frac{\partial\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}}{\partial\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{j_{x}}}\right)={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}_{\mathrm{n}_{{}_{x}}}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{k_{x}}\mathcal{S}_{i_{x}j_{x}}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{l_{x}}

(214)

and then

{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}^{k_{x}l_{x}}\left(\frac{\partial\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}}{\partial\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{i_{x}}}\right)\left(\frac{\partial\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}}{\partial\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{j_{x}}}\right)=2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}_{\mathrm{n}_{{}_{x}}}\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}

(215)

A. Repulsive Force Conjecture (RFC):

Given a massive particle state in the
EFT
3D
of mass
M_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}
, the RFC which in the infinite distance limit coincides
with the asymptotic WGC, requires the following inequality
F_{Coulomb}\geq F_{Grav}+F_{Yukawa}.
This inequality reads explicitly as follows

\frac{\mathrm{g}_{{\small 3D}}^{2}}{M_{\mathrm{Pl}}}\left(q_{\text{{a}}}\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}q_{\text{{b}}}\right)\geq\left.\frac{D-3}{D-2}\right|_{D=3}\frac{M_{\mathrm{k}}^{2}}{M_{\mathrm{Pl}}^{2}}+\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}}}_{{\small QG}}+\mathcal{Y}

(216)

with Yukawa matter coupling contribution
\mathcal{Y}
reading in terms of
the reduced mass
\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}
as follows

\mathcal{Y}=\frac{\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}}{2\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}}\left(\frac{\partial\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}}{\partial\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{a}}}}\right)\left(\frac{\partial\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}}{\partial\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{b}}}}\right)-\frac{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 293$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 293$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 293$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 293$}}}}{\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}}\left[\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{a}}}\frac{\partial\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}}{\partial\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{a}}}}\right]^{2}

(217)

with
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 293$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 293$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 293$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 293$}}}=1/2
. Notice that as for the towers of BPS states and because
here
D=3,
the classical gravity contribution
\left(D-3\right)/\left(D-2\right)
to the RFC vanishes identically; however one expects a non
trivial
negative
contribution
\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}}}_{{\small QG}}
of
the vacuum coming from quantum effect.

Substituting into (
216
), the RFC inequality for non BPS states becomes

\frac{\mathrm{g}_{{\small 3D}}^{2}}{M_{\mathrm{Pl}}}\left(q_{\text{{a}}}\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}q_{\text{{b}}}\right)\geq\frac{\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}}{2\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}}\left(\frac{\partial\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}}{\partial\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{a}}}}\right)\left(\frac{\partial\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}}{\partial\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{b}}}}\right)-\frac{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 293$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 293$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 293$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 293$}}}}{\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}}4\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{4}+\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}}}_{{\small QG}}^{\prime}

(218)

where we have used
L_{0}\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}=2\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}.
Multiplying both sides by
\left(-1\right),
we
bring this inequality to

-\frac{\mathrm{g}_{{\small 3D}}^{2}}{M_{\mathrm{Pl}}}\left(q_{\text{{a}}}\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}q_{\text{{b}}}\right)\leq\left(4{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 293$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 293$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 293$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 293$}}}\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}-\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}}}_{{\small QG}}\right)-\frac{\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}}{2\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}}\left(\frac{\partial\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}}{\partial\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{a}}}}\right)\left(\frac{\partial\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}}{\partial\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{b}}}}\right)

and then into the following stronger form

\framebox{ $\left.\begin{array}[]{c}\text{ }\\
\text{ }\end{array}\right.$\ \ $-\frac{\mathrm{g}_{{\small 3D}}^{2}}{M_{\mathrm{Pl}}}\left(q_{\text{{a}}}\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}q_{\text{{b}}}\right)\leq 4{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 293$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 293$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 293$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 293$}}}\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}-\frac{\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}}{2\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}}\left(\frac{\partial\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}}{\partial\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{a}}}}\right)\left(\frac{\partial\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{x}}^{2}}{\partial\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{\text{{b}}}}\right)$ \ \ \ \ \ }

(219)

just because
\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}}}_{{\small QG}}<0.

B. Computing left hand side of eq(
219
):

The LHS term of eq(
219
) is given by the term
-\frac{\mathrm{g}_{{\small 3D}}^{2}}{M_{\mathrm{Pl}}}\left(q_{\text{{a}}}\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}q_{\text{{b}}}\right);
depending on whether the
charge
q_{\text{{a}}}
is given by the fibral value
q_{i_{\bot}}\neq 0
with
q_{a_{\|}}=0;
or the base one namely
q_{a_{\|}}\neq 0
with
q_{i_{\bot}}=0,
we split this contribution in two blocs as
follows

fiber K3
⊥

:

-\frac{\mathrm{g}_{{\small 3D}}^{2}}{M_{\mathrm{Pl}}}\left(q_{i_{\bot}}\mathcal{G}^{i_{\bot}j_{\bot}}q_{j_{\bot}}\right)

base K3
∥

:

-\frac{\mathrm{g}_{{\small 3D}}^{2}}{M_{\mathrm{Pl}}}\left(q_{a_{\|}}\mathcal{G}^{a_{\|}b_{\|}}q_{b_{\|}}\right)

(220)

We do the calculation for the fiber K3
{}_{\bot};
and deduce the result for
the base by using the automorphism symmetry permuting K3
⊥
and K3
∥
(Weak/Strong duality).

Using eqs(
178
-
181
), we can solve the relation
\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ac}}}\mathcal{G}_{\text{{cb}}}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{\text{{b}}}^{\text{{a}}}
as

\mathcal{G}_{\text{{ab}}}

=

\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{1}{\mathcal{\hat{V}}_{K3_{\|}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{a_{\|}b_{\|}}&0\\
0&\frac{1}{\mathcal{\hat{V}}_{K3_{\perp}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{i_{\perp}j_{\perp}}\end{array}\right)

=

\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathcal{\hat{V}}_{K3_{\perp}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{a_{\|}b_{\|}}&0\\
0&\mathcal{\hat{V}}_{K3_{\|}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{i_{\perp}j_{\perp}}\end{array}\right)

\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}

=

\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathcal{\hat{V}}_{K3_{\|}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}^{a_{\|}b_{\|}}&0\\
0&\mathcal{\hat{V}}_{K3_{\perp}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}^{i_{\perp}j_{\perp}}\end{array}\right)

=

\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{1}{\mathcal{\hat{V}}_{K3_{\perp}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}^{a_{\|}b_{\|}}&0\\
0&\frac{1}{\mathcal{\hat{V}}_{K3_{\|}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}^{i_{\perp}j_{\perp}}\end{array}\right)

(221)

with

\mathcal{\hat{V}}_{{\small K3}_{{\small\bot}}}

=

\frac{1}{2}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{i_{\perp}j_{\perp}}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{i_{\perp}}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{j_{\perp}}

\mathcal{\hat{V}}_{{\small K3}_{\|}}

=

\frac{1}{2}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{a_{\|}b_{\|}}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{a_{\|}}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{b_{\|}}

(222)

and scaling behaviours in the infinite distance limit like
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{-2}\mathcal{\hat{V}}_{{\small K3}_{{\small\bot}}}
; and
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{+2}\mathcal{\hat{V}}_{{\small K3}_{\|}}.

Using these values, the LHS in (

) reads as

\displaystyle-\frac{\mathrm{g}_{{\small 3D}}^{2}}{M_{\mathrm{Pl}}}\left(q_{i_{\bot}}\mathcal{G}^{i_{\perp}j_{\perp}}q_{j_{\bot}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\mathrm{g}_{{\small 3D}}^{2}}{M_{\mathrm{Pl}}}\mathcal{\hat{V}}_{K3_{\perp}}\mathbf{q}_{\bot}^{2}

(223)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2\mathrm{g}_{{\small 3D}}^{2}}{M_{\mathrm{Pl}}}\mathcal{\hat{V}}_{K3_{\perp}}\mathrm{n}_{\bot}

where we have used
\mathbf{q}_{\bot}^{2}=\left(q_{i_{\bot}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}^{i_{\perp}j_{\perp}}q_{j_{\bot}}\right)
and
\mathbf{q}_{\bot}^{2}=-2\mathrm{n}_{\bot}.
Thus, the contribution of the LHS is given by

\left.\begin{array}[]{c}\text{ }\\
\text{ }\end{array}\right.

\ \ LHS_{\bot}=-\frac{\mathrm{g}_{{\small 3D}}^{2}}{M_{\mathrm{Pl}}}\left(q_{i_{\bot}}\mathcal{G}^{i_{\perp}j_{\perp}}q_{j_{\bot}}\right)=\frac{2\mathrm{g}_{{\small 3D}}^{2}}{M_{\mathrm{Pl}}}\mathcal{\hat{V}}_{{\small K3}_{{\small\bot}}}
n
⊥

\left.\begin{array}[]{c}\text{ }\\
\text{ }\end{array}\right.

\ \ LHS_{\|}=-\frac{\mathrm{g}_{{\small 3D}}^{2}}{M_{\mathrm{Pl}}}\left(q_{i_{\|}}\mathcal{G}^{a_{\|}b_{\|}}q_{j_{\|}}\right)=\frac{2\mathrm{g}_{{\small 3D}}^{2}}{M_{\mathrm{Pl}}}\mathcal{\hat{V}}_{{\small K3}_{\|}}
n
∥

(225)

These
LHS_{\bot}
and the
LHS_{\|}
are manifestly related under
Weak/Strong duality generated by the transposition
K3_{\bot}\leftrightarrow K3_{\|}.

B. Computing right hand side of (

)

The contribution in the RHS of eq(

) for the fiber K3
⟂
is
given by

RHS_{\perp}=\left(2\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{\perp}}^{2}+\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}}}_{{\small QG}}\right)-\frac{\mathcal{\hat{V}}_{K3_{\perp}}}{2\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{\perp}}^{2}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}^{i_{\perp}j_{\perp}}\frac{\partial\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{\perp}}^{2}}{\partial\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{i_{\perp}}}\frac{\partial\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{\perp}}^{2}}{\partial\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{j_{\perp}}}

(226)

where we have substituted
\mathcal{G}^{i_{\perp}j_{\perp}}=\mathcal{\hat{V}}_{K3_{\perp}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}^{i_{\perp}j_{\perp}}.

\frac{\partial\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{\perp}}^{2}}{\partial\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{i_{\perp}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}^{i_{\perp}j_{\perp}}\frac{\partial\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{\perp}}^{2}}{\partial\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{j_{x}}}=2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}_{\mathrm{n}_{{}_{\perp}}}\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{\perp}}^{2}

(227)

the above expression reduces down to

RHS_{\perp}=\left(2\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{\perp}}^{2}+\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}}}_{{\small QG}}-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}_{\mathrm{n}_{{}_{\perp}}}\mathcal{\hat{V}}_{K3_{\perp}}\right)

2\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{n}_{\perp}}^{2}=2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}_{\mathrm{n}_{{}_{\perp}}}\mathcal{\hat{V}}_{K3_{x}}+\frac{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}_{\mathrm{n}_{{}_{\perp}}}}{2\mathrm{n}_{{}_{\perp}}}\left(q_{i_{\perp}}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{i_{\perp}}\right)^{2}

(229)

and putting into (

), we get

RHS_{\perp}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}_{\mathrm{n}_{{}_{{}_{\perp}}}}\left[\mathcal{\hat{V}}_{K3_{\perp}}+\frac{1}{2\mathrm{n}_{{}_{{}_{\perp}}}}\left(q_{i_{{}_{\perp}}}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}}^{i_{{}_{\perp}}}\right)^{2}\right]

It is indeed greater than to the contribution of left hand sides

n
x
( with

) in agreement with the repulsive force conjecture (

).

6
Conclusion and discussions

The WGC has been definitively one of the most studied conjectures of
the Swampland; it has undergone numerous refinements and generalisations
such as the one investigated in this paper for the
EFT
3D
.
Here, we took a particular interest in the Repulsive
Force Conjecture (RFC), the tower WGC and its most outstanding derivative
the Asymptotic WGC, where we studied different regimes of gauge couplings,
weak and strong. First, we deepened the study in the weak coupling regime of
the
EFT
3D
descending from M-theory on the family of
Calabi-Yau fourfold
K3_{\bot}\times K3_{\|};
and constructed the
towers of BPS or non-BPS states satisfying the Asymptotic WGC. Then, we used
the discrete symmetry of the CY4 generated by the permutation
K3_{\bot}\leftrightarrow K3_{\|}
and showed through explicit calculations the
existence of a dual version of the Asymptotic WGC. This dual description
involves strong gauge coupling dual to the weak gauge coupling investigated
in
12
for M-theory on CY3 with K3 fibration.

In order to classify the infinite distance limits in M-theory on Calabi-Yau
fourfolds, we use the so called type-
\mathbb{T}
and type-
\mathbb{S}

studied in
11A
. For generic
EFT
{}_{{\small 3D}},
we
showed that there exists three possible classes termed type-
\mathbb{T}
,
type-
\mathbb{S}
(as in
EFT
5D
) in addition to
type-
\mathbb{V}
for
EFT
3D
. These represent
respectively a
X_{4}
with an elliptic fiber, a surface fiber, and a volume
fiber; they correspond to the internal manifolds
X_{4}
taking the form
\mathcal{F}_{n}\times\mathcal{B}_{n-4}
with
n=1,2,3
being the complex
dimension of the fiber. Thus, we constructed a Calabi-Yau fourfold with
shrinking fibers and diverging bases, keeping the total volume of the
manifold finite (
\mathcal{V}_{X_{4}}<\infty
) to insure the dynamics of
gravity; this allowed us to probe infinite distance in the moduli space
which correspond to weak coupling regime (
1
).

In fact to make contact with this physical aspect, we compactified M-theory
on the aforementioned manifold, where we derived the 3D action from the 11D
supergravity action with M-brane. Then, we discussed the weak gauge coupling
limit that constitutes half of our main focus in this paper; the other half
regards the strong gauge regime. This has been motivated by the type-
\mathbb{S}
form of the CY4 fibration. For this surface form, the Calabi-Yau
fourfold is fibered as
S_{\perp}\times S_{\parallel}
with finite total
volume
\mathcal{V}_{X_{4}}=\mathcal{V}_{S_{\perp}}\mathcal{V}_{S_{\|}};
with
\left(\mathbf{i}\right)
a fibral surface volume
\mathcal{V}_{S_{\perp}}
shrinking in the infinite distance limit as
\mathcal{O}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{-2}\right);
and
\left(\mathbf{ii}\right)

base surface volume
\mathcal{V}_{S_{\|}}
expanding in the infinite
distance limit as
\mathcal{O}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{+2}\right).
Because the
S_{\perp}
and the
S_{\parallel}
have some complex dimension, the CY4
has a discrete
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry given by transposition of the roles
of
S_{\perp}
and
S_{\parallel};
that is
S_{\perp}\leftrightarrow S_{\parallel}.
This discrete duality induces a Weak/Strong duality between
weak and strong gauge couplings generated by the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow 1/{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}.
In the present study, we took the
interesting CY4 geometry
X_{4}=K3_{\perp}\times K3_{\parallel}
and we
showed that the full abelian gauge symmetry of the
EFT
3D

factorises like
U\left(1\right)_{\bot}^{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}}\times U\left(1\right)_{\|}^{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}}
as in (
107
). This CY4 is of particular interest
due to the intrinsic properties of
K3
where several calculations can be
performed explicitly. Using the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
duality, we can switch from
the fiber to the base using the mapping
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow 1/{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}
;
and then switch from weak to strong gauge groups. This is an interesting
property for the study of the Asymptotic WGC in
EFT
3D

and its test as done explicitly in section 5; it also predicts a
strong coupling regime with a dual Asymptotic WGC which can be
interpreted as a strong version of Asymptotic WGC. There, towers
\mathcal{T}_{\bot}^{\text{{bps}}}
of BPS and towers
\mathcal{T}_{\bot}^{\text{{n-bps}}}
of non BPS states with light masses have
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
duals given by towers
\mathcal{T}_{\|}^{\text{{bps}}}
of
BPS and towers
\mathcal{T}_{\|}^{\text{{n-bps}}}
of non BPS
states with heavy masses. These towers of BPS and non BPS particle states
in the
EFT
3D
are described in the two claims of
subsection 4.3.

To insure the viability of the conjecture in 3d gravity induced by
M-theory on a Calabi-Yau fourfold of the form
X_{4}=K3_{\perp}\times K3_{\parallel}
we thoroughly tested the Swampland conjecture in
both weakly and strongly coupled regimes in section 5
.
This
successful test is motivating the use of this internal geometry to probe
other conjectures of the Swampland. In this regard, one can mention in
particular the Distance Conjecture and the No-Global Symmetries Conjecture.
The importance of such investigations lies in the possibility of
unifying the Swampland conjectures in such a way that one could
reduce the number of conjectures to the most fundamental ones. Progress in
this direction will be reported in a future occasion.

Appendix A
Appendix: Towers of BPS/non BPS states

In this appendix, we give useful details regarding the BPS and the
non-BPS towers in the K3 fibered Calabi-Yau fourfold
X_{4}=K3\times K3
as
in the Figure
1
. We also comment on the existence of
asymptotic light (resp. heavy) towers of particle states in the weak (resp.
strong) coupling regime associated with the infinite (resp. short ) distance
limit where the K3 fiber shrinks (resp. diverges) and the base expands
(resp. shrinks) such that the CY4 volume
\mathcal{V}_{X_{4}}
is finite.

M-theory on
K3_{\bot}\times K3_{\|}
in weak/strong gauge
regimes

In the investigation of the underlying 3D effective field theory (
EFT
3D
) in weak (strong) regime descending from M-theory
on
K3_{\bot}\times K3_{\|}
with finite volume
\mathcal{V}_{X_{4}}\sim\mathcal{V}_{K3_{\bot}}\mathcal{V}_{K3_{\|}},
one has to
distinguish between different classes of fibers depending on whether the K3
fiber (base) is a regular surface, or it has singularities at isolated
points on the base (fiber) occurring
\left(i\right)
either at finite
distances in the moduli space (Kulikov of type I); or
\left(ii\right)
at
the infinite distances (Kulikov of type II and III)
27A
;
27B
. Below, we focus on regular K3s at infinite distances with finite volume of
the CY4.

Given a basis set of basic holomorphic curves
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}}}}=\{C^{\text{{a}}}\}_{1\leq\text{{a}}\leq\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}}}\leq h_{{\small X}_{{\small 4}}}^{{\small 1,1}}}
generating a subgroup of H
{}_{2}\left(X_{4}\right),
one associates several interesting quantities in
the QFT
3D
descending from the compactified M-theory on
X_{4}:

\bullet

Gauge coupling regimes

Under compactification, one induces a basis set of abelian 1-form gauge
potentials {
A^{\text{{a}}}
}
{}_{1\leq\text{{a}}\leq\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}}}}
. The 1-forms
A^{\text{{a}}}
are given by the wrapping
\int_{C^{\text{{a}}}}\boldsymbol{C}_{3}
of the 3-form potential
\boldsymbol{C}_{3}
of the M-theory on
C^{\text{{a}}}
; the
A^{\text{{a}}}
’s couple to M2/
C^{\text{{a}}}
. Together with
the basis set of gauge potentials {
A^{\text{{a}}}
}, we have the
abelian gauge symmetry group
G=\prod\nolimits_{\text{{a}}}U(1)^{\text{{a}}}
with gauge coupling matrix
\mathcal{G}_{\text{{ab}}}=\int_{X_{4}}J_{\text{{a}}}\wedge\ast J_{\text{{b}}}.

This coupling matrix decomposes on
K3_{\bot}\times K3_{\|}
in terms
of the Kahler 2-forms
J_{\text{{a}}}=(J_{a_{\|}},J_{i_{\bot}})

and its Hodge dual
\ast J_{\text{{a}}}=(\ast J_{a_{\|}},\ast J_{i_{\bot}})
as

\mathcal{G}_{a_{\|}b_{\|}}

=

\displaystyle\int\nolimits_{X_{4}}J_{a_{\|}}\wedge\ast J_{b_{\|}}

,

\mathcal{G}_{a_{\|}j_{\bot}}

=

\displaystyle\int\nolimits_{X_{4}}J_{a_{\|}}\wedge\ast J_{j_{\bot}}

\mathcal{G}_{i_{\bot}j_{\bot}}

=

\displaystyle\int\nolimits_{X_{4}}J_{i_{\bot}}\wedge\ast J_{j_{\bot}}

,

\mathcal{G}_{i_{\bot}b_{\|}}

=

\displaystyle\int\nolimits_{X_{4}}J_{i_{\bot}}\wedge\ast J_{b_{\|}}

(232)

with leading dependence in the spectral parameter
\textstyle\mathchar 277
like

\mathcal{G}_{\text{{ab}}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}}\right)&\mathcal{O}\left(1\right)\\
\mathcal{O}\left(1\right)&\mathcal{O}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}\right)\end{array}\right)\qquad\underrightarrow{\text{ \ \ }{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\rightarrow\infty\text{
\ \ \ }}\qquad\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&1\\
1&\infty\end{array}\right)

(233)

Using results from the core of the paper, the inverse gauge metric
\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}
denoted as follows

\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathcal{G}^{a_{\|}b_{\|}}&\mathcal{G}^{a_{\|}j_{\bot}}\\
\mathcal{G}^{i_{\bot}b_{\|}}&\mathcal{G}^{i_{\bot}j_{\bot}}\end{array}\right)

(234)

behaves in the infinite distance limit as

\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathcal{O}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}\right)&\mathcal{O}\left(1\right)\\
\mathcal{O}\left(1\right)&\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}^{2}}\right)\end{array}\right)\qquad\rightarrow\qquad\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\infty&1\\
1&0\end{array}\right)

(235)

indicating the existence of two gauge regimes: weak and strong, and
suggesting the existence of holomorphic curves
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}
in
the CY4 where sit weak and gauge 3D effective field theories.

\bullet

Complex curves
\Upsilon_{weak}
and
\Upsilon_{strong}

Generic complex curves
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}
inside the
X_{4}
are given
by the expansion
\sum_{\text{{a}}{\small=1}}^{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}}}q_{\text{{a}}}C^{\text{{a}}}
; they carry an integral charge vector
\mathbf{q}
under the gauge symmetry
G
with components
\left(q_{\text{{a}}}\right)
splitting along the fiber and the base directions like

\left(q_{a_{\|}},q_{i_{\bot}}\right)
. These generic curves support
the following objects:

\left(\mathbf{i}\right)
the 1-form gauge potential
A_{\mathbf{q}}=\int_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}}\boldsymbol{C}_{3}
which by substituting
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}=

\sum_{\text{{a}}{\small=1}}^{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}}}q_{\text{{a}}}C^{\text{{a}}}
expands in turn as a linear
combination of the individual gauge potential as follows
\sum_{\text{{a}}{\small=1}}^{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}}}q_{\text{{a}}}A^{\text{{a}}}
.

\left(\mathbf{ii}\right)
the abelian Maxwell-like gauge symmetry
U(1)_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}}
is given by the linear combination
\sum_{\text{{a}}{\small=1}}^{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}}}q_{\text{{a}}}U(1)^{\text{{a}}}
. This expansion has two contribution like
U(1)_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\|}}}+U(1)_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\bot}}}
; the bloc
U(1)_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\|}}}
originates from the base surface and
the
U(1)_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\bot}}}
from the fiber,

U(1)_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\|}}}=\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{a_{\|}=1}^{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}}_{\|}}q_{a_{\|}}U(1)^{a_{\|}}\qquad,\qquad U(1)_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}_{\bot}}}=\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{i_{\bot}=1}^{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}}_{\bot}}q_{i_{\bot}}U(1)^{i_{\bot}}

(236)

\left(\mathbf{iii}\right)
An effective field theory
EFT
3D
located on a given complex curve
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}
has a gauge
symmetry
U(1)_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}}
. Its gauge coupling constant
denoted as g
{}_{{}_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}}}
is a function of the 3D
gauge coupling
\mathrm{g}_{{\small YM}}
and the integral charges
q_{\text{{a}}}.
The squared g
{}_{{}_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}}}^{2}
is
given the relation
\mathrm{g}_{{\small YM}}^{2}q_{\text{{a}}}\mathcal{G}^{\text{{ab}}}q_{\text{{b}}}
. Moreover, because of
the behaviour (
235
) in the spectral parameter
\textstyle\mathchar 277
, we
distinguish two interesting gauge regime limits: weak and strong given by

weak regime

:

\mathrm{g}_{{}_{weak}}^{2}

\sim

q_{i_{\bot}}\mathcal{G}^{i_{\bot}j_{\bot}}q_{j_{\bot}}

strong regime

:

\mathrm{g}_{{}_{strong}}^{2}

\sim

q_{a_{\|}}\mathcal{G}^{a_{\|}b_{\|}}q_{b_{\|}}

(237)

with

\mathcal{G}^{i_{\bot}j_{\bot}}=\frac{1}{\mathcal{V}_{K3_{\|}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}^{i_{\bot}j_{\bot}},\qquad\mathcal{G}^{a_{\|}b_{\|}}=\frac{1}{\mathcal{V}_{K3_{\bot}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}^{a_{\|}b_{\|}}

(238)

where
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}^{i_{\bot}j_{\bot}}
and
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}^{a_{\|}b_{\|}}
are
respectively the metrics of the hyperbolic sublattices
\Lambda_{\bot}^{\ast}
and
\Lambda_{\|}^{\ast}
and where
\mathcal{V}_{K3_{\bot}}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{i_{\bot}j_{\bot}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{i_{\bot}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{j_{\bot}}/2

and
\mathcal{V}_{K3_{\|}}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{a_{\|}b_{\|}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{a_{\|}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}^{b_{\|}}/2
are the volumes of the two
K3
s.
These weak and strong gauge regimes are supported by the curves
\Upsilon_{weak}
and
\Upsilon_{strong}
given by the expansions

\Upsilon_{weak}=\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{i_{\bot}=1}^{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}}_{\bot}}q_{i_{\bot}}\mathcal{C}^{i_{\bot}}\qquad,\qquad\Upsilon_{strong}=\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{a_{\|}=1}^{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}}_{\|}}q_{a_{\|}}\mathcal{C}^{a_{\|}}

(239)

Here the set {
\mathcal{C}^{i_{\bot}}
} generates a hyperbolic-like
sublattice
\Lambda_{\bot}^{\ast}
with signature (1,
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}}_{\bot}-1
);
it is contained into the 22 dimensional K3
⊥
lattice
\Gamma_{\bot}^{3,19}.
Similarly, the set {
\mathcal{C}^{a_{\|}}
} generates also
a hyperbolic lattice
\Lambda_{\|}^{\ast}
with signature (
1,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}}_{\|}-1
); it is a sublattice of the 22 dimensional lattice
\Gamma_{\|}^{3,19}.

Notice that if the fiber K3
⊥
(resp. the base K3
∥
) does
not degenerate on the base K3
∥
(resp. the fiber K3
⊥
), the
divisors
\mathcal{D}_{i_{\bot}}
(resp.
\mathcal{D}_{\|}
) are given
by fibering the
C^{i_{\bot}}
over K3
∥
(resp.
\mathcal{C}^{a_{\|}}
over K3
⊥
). Moreover, if
\mathfrak{i}:K3\rightarrow X_{4}
is an embedding in
X_{4}
; then the image of the Piccard group
Pic(K3) of generic K3 is given by
H^{2}(X_{4},\mathbb{Z})\cap H^{1,1}(X_{4}).

\bullet

BPS and anti-BPS curves

The the full charge lattice of the regular CY4 considered in our study is
given by
\Gamma_{\|}^{3,19}\oplus\Gamma_{\bot}^{3,19}
with the 22
dimensional lattice
\Gamma^{3,19}
being the lattice H
{}^{2}\left(K3,\mathbb{Z}\right)
decomposing into blocks like

\Gamma_{\|}^{3,19}=U_{\|}^{{\small\oplus}3}\oplus E_{8\|}\oplus E_{8\|}\qquad,\qquad U_{\bot}^{{\small\oplus 3}}\oplus(E_{8\bot}\oplus E_{8\bot})

(240)

with
U
standing for the usual 2D hyperbolic lattice, and each copy
E_{8}

representing the root lattice of exceptional
E_{8}
Lie algebra. The 22+22
dimensional lattice
\Gamma_{\|}^{3,19}\oplus\Gamma_{\bot}^{3,19}

contains the sublattice
\Lambda^{\ast}{\small[{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}}]}=\Lambda_{\|}^{\ast}{\small[{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}}_{{}_{\|}}]}\oplus\Lambda_{\bot}^{\ast}{\small[{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}}_{{}_{\bot}}]}
with total dimension
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}}_{\bot}+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}}_{\|}
and partial
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}}_{\bot},{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}}_{\|}

\leq 20.
Furthermore, the
fibral sublattice
\Lambda_{\bot}^{\ast}
decomposes into a self and
anti-self dual sublattices like
\Lambda_{\bot+}^{\ast}\oplus\Lambda_{\bot-}^{\ast}
in the sense that charge vectors
\mathbf{q}_{\bot}=\sum_{i_{\bot}=1}^{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}}_{\bot}}q_{i_{\bot}}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 290$}}}}^{i_{\bot}}
splits also as

\mathbf{q}_{\bot}=\mathbf{q}_{\bot+}\oplus\mathbf{q}_{\bot-}

(241)

In this parameterisation, the metric
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}^{i_{\bot}j_{\bot}}=\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 290$}}}}^{i_{\bot}}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 290$}}}}^{j_{\bot}}
has an
indefinite sign [type hyperbolic]; the number
\mathbf{q}_{\bot+}^{2}
is
positive definite while the
\mathbf{q}_{\bot-}^{2}
is negative definite.

Similarly, the base sublattice
\Lambda_{\|}^{\ast}
decomposes also
into a self dual
\Lambda_{\|+}^{\ast}
and anti-self dual
\Lambda_{\|-}^{\ast}
sublattices. The charge vector
\mathbf{q}_{\|}=\sum_{a_{\|}=1}^{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}}_{\|}}q_{a_{\|}}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 290$}}}}^{a_{\|}}
with hyperbolic- like metric
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}^{a_{\|}b_{\|}}=\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 290$}}}}^{a_{\|}}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 290$}}}}^{b_{\|}}

splits as well as

\mathbf{q}_{\|}=\mathbf{q}_{\|+}\oplus\mathbf{q}_{\|-}

(242)

with
\mathbf{q}_{\|+}^{2}
positive and
\mathbf{q}_{\|-}^{2}

negative definite. Because of the property
\mathbf{q}^{2}=|\mathbf{q}_{+}|^{2}-|\mathbf{q}_{-}|^{2},
we see that there are three region in the
charge lattice depending on the sign of
\mathbf{q}^{2}
. So, depending on
the region of
\Lambda_{\|}^{\ast}\oplus\Lambda_{\bot}^{\ast}

where the curve
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}
is located, we distinguish
different types of particle states in the
EFT
{}_{{\small 3D}}.
These
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}
’s are given by movable curves inside K3 and non
movable ones as described below.

Movable curves in Mori cone of K3

These are holomorphic curves
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}=\sum_{\text{{a}}{\small=1}}^{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 282$}}}}q_{\text{{a}}}C^{\text{{a}}}
that have
positive self intersection (
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}^{2}>0
); that is charge
as
\mathbf{q}^{2}>0.
This condition cane be
\left(i\right)
either

simply
solved by charges
\mathbf{q=q}_{+}
sitting in the self dual
\Lambda_{+}^{\ast},
or
\left(ii\right)
generally like
\mathbf{q}=\mathbf{q}_{+}+\mathbf{q}_{-}
with the condition
|\mathbf{q}_{+}|^{2}>|\mathbf{q}_{-}|^{2}.
They lie inside the movable cone of K3, and they
define rays
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{Q}_{n}}=n\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}
with
positive integer n generating a tower of BPS particle states with charges
\mathbf{Q}_{n}=n\mathbf{q}
. These particle states, formally denoted as

M2/\Upsilon_{\mathbf{Q}_{n}}

(243)

are given by wrapping a M2 brane on
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{Q}_{n}}
and have
interesting features; in particular: They are remarkably associated with a
non vanishing genus zero BPS invariants
\mathcal{N}_{n\Sigma}^{0}
(often
termed as Gopakumar-Vafa invariant
\mathcal{N}_{n\Sigma}^{0}\neq 0
with
integer
n\geq 1
). These BPS invariants are determined by the expansion
coefficients
c\left(m\right)
of a meromorphic modular form
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}}\left(q\right)
of weight
-2

30
;
31
namely

{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}}\left(q\right)=\sum_{m\geq-1}c\left(m\right)e^{i2m{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}}\text{\qquad},\text{\qquad}c\left(m\right)\neq 0

(244)

with the relation

\mathcal{N}_{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{Q}_{n}}}^{0}=c\left(\Upsilon_{\mathbf{Q}_{n}}^{2}/2\right)\text{\qquad}\Rightarrow\text{\qquad}\Upsilon_{\mathbf{Q}_{n}}^{2}\geq-2

(245)

The BPS states are also characterised by the so-called Gromov-Witten (GW)
invariants
32
and by Noether-Leftshetz (NL) numbers
32
;
33
for which all curve classes [
\Upsilon
] with the similar
intersection form and discriminant class in
\Lambda^{\ast}
have the same
invariant.

The BPS particle states of this
EFT
3D
have as well an
interesting realisation in terms of mode excitations of the
heterotic

string on
\mathbb{T}^{5}\times K3.
This string comes by using two
dualities: first the duality between M-theory on
K3\times K3\times\mathbb{S}_{M}^{1}
and Type IIA on
K3\times K3
; and second the duality between the
Type IIA string on
K3
and the heterotic string on
\mathbb{T}^{4}
.

Furthermore, because here we have two K3 surfaces (the fiber
K3_{\bot}

and the base
K3_{\|}
) in the Calabi-Yau compactification of M-theory
down to 3D, we can distinguish four sectors of K3 curves depending on their
location in
K3_{\bot}\times K3_{\|}
; these sectors are given by the
pairs

\left(\Upsilon_{\bot+},\Upsilon_{\|+}\right),\qquad\left(\Upsilon_{\bot+},\Upsilon_{\|-}\right),\qquad\left(\Upsilon_{\bot-},\Upsilon_{\|+}\right),\qquad\left(\Upsilon_{\bot-},\Upsilon_{\|-}\right)

(246)

This splitting goes with the existence of two kinds of dual
heterotic

strings for M-theory on
K3_{\bot}\times K3_{\|}
due to the
automorphism symmetry
K3_{\bot}\leftrightarrow K3_{\|}
. The dual
strings are given by heterotic string on
\mathbb{S}_{M}^{1}\times\mathbb{T}^{4}\times K3_{\bot}
and the heterotic on
K3_{\|}\times\mathbb{S}_{M}^{1}\times\mathbb{T}^{4}.

Non movable curves in K3

These are rigid curves
\Sigma
living in the charge sublattice
\Lambda_{\|}^{\ast}\oplus\Lambda_{\bot}^{\ast}
of the Calabi-Yau fourfold

K3_{\bot}\times K3_{\|}.
They lie outside the movable cone
of the K3 surfaces and do not support BPS states. These holomorphic curves
have negative self intersection (
\Sigma^{2}<0
), and they define a ray
\Sigma=\mathrm{k}\Sigma
labeled by the integer k and the
generator
\Sigma
. This ray in the charge lattice defines a tower
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\text{{n-bps}}}
of non-BPS particle
states. Examples of such curves
\Sigma
are given by the hyperbolic family

\Sigma=l_{{\small U}}\mathcal{C}^{{\small U}}-l_{{\small T}}\mathcal{C}^{{\small T}}\qquad\leftrightarrow\qquad\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}=q_{{\small 1}}\mathcal{C}^{1}+q_{{\small 2}}\mathcal{C}^{2}

(247)

parameterised by two non zero integers
l_{{\small U}}
and
l_{{\small T}}

having the same sign (i.e:
l_{{\small U}}.l_{{\small T}}>0
). These curves
are generated by two 2-cycles
\mathcal{C}^{{\small U}}
and
\mathcal{C}^{{\small T}}
sitting inside K3 and realised in terms of a 2-torus
\mathbb{T}^{2}
and a projective line
\mathbb{P}^{1}
(2-sphere) as follows

\mathcal{C}^{{\small S}}=\mathbb{P}^{1},\qquad\mathcal{C}^{{\small T}}=\mathbb{T}^{2},\qquad\mathcal{C}^{{\small U}}=\mathbb{T}^{2}+\mathbb{P}^{1}

(248)

with the intersections

\mathcal{C}^{{\small T}}.\mathcal{C}^{{\small S}}=1,\qquad\ \mathcal{C}^{{\small T}}.\mathcal{C}^{{\small T}}=0,\qquad\mathcal{C}^{{\small S}}.\mathcal{C}^{{\small S}}=-2

(249)

From these relations, we learn the following features: First, the self
intersections
\mathcal{C}^{{\small U}}.\mathcal{C}^{{\small U}}=0
and

\Sigma.\Sigma=-2l_{{\small U}}l_{{\small T}}<0\qquad\leftrightarrow\qquad\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}.\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}.=\mathbf{q}^{2}<0

(250)

Second, the curves
\Sigma
can be put in correspondence
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}=\sum q_{\text{{a}}}\mathcal{C}^{\text{{a}}}
with
self intersection
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}.\Upsilon_{\mathbf{q}}.=\mathbf{q}^{2};
this intersection factorises like
\mathbf{q}^{2}=2q_{{\small 1}}q_{{\small 2}}<0
and reads in terms of the off diagonal
1
1

1

Hyperbolic diagonal metric is generated by taking a basis the two
2-cycles
\mathcal{C}_{+}=\mathcal{C}^{{\small U}}+\mathcal{C}^{{\small T}}

and
\mathcal{C}_{-}=\mathcal{C}^{{\small U}}-\mathcal{C}^{{\small T}}
.
Their intersection matrix is
\mathcal{C}_{+}.\mathcal{C}_{+}=+2
and
\mathcal{C}_{-}.\mathcal{C}_{-}=-2
as well as
\mathcal{C}_{+}.\mathcal{C}_{-}=0.
hyperbolic metric like
q_{\text{{a}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}^{\text{{ab}}}q_{\text{{b}}}
with
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}^{\text{{aa}}}=0
and
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}^{12}=1.
In the 2-cycle basis {
\mathbb{P}^{1},\mathbb{T}^{2}
}, the
generic complex curve
\Sigma
expands like
l_{{\small U}}\mathbb{P}^{1}+\left(l_{{\small U}}-l_{{\small T}}\right)\mathbb{T}^{2}
and reduces
for the particular case where
l_{{\small U}}=l_{{\small T}}=l
to the
multiple curve

\Sigma_{l}=l\mathbb{P}^{1}

(251)

with self intersection
\Sigma.\Sigma=-2l^{2}.

In the charge lattice
\Lambda^{\ast}
generated by {
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 290$}}}}^{\text{{a}}}
} and metric
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}^{\text{{ab}}}=\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 290$}}}}^{\text{{a}}}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 290$}}}}^{\text{{a}}},

the particle states of the 3D effective gauge theory with gauge symmetry
U\left(1\right)^{2}
are labeled by two integers
\left(q_{{\small 1}},q_{{\small 2}}\right).
It turns out that these integers can be interpreted in
terms of the left moving excitations
n_{L}
in the dual heterotic string
description of M-theory on
K3_{\bot}\times K3_{\|}
. There, the
integer
l_{{\small U}}
is seen as the string winding modes (
n_{\text{{w}}}=q_{{\small 1}}),
and the integer
l_{{\small T}}
just the KK
modes (
n_{\text{{KK}}}=q_{{\small 2}}
); and their product
q_{{\small 1}}q_{{\small 2}}
is related to the moving excitation number as

n_{L}=-q_{{\small 1}}q_{{\small 2}}\qquad,\qquad n_{L}=-\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{q}^{2}

(252)

Recall that M-theory on
K3\times\mathbb{B}_{2}\times\mathbb{S}_{M}^{1}

is dual to Type IIA string on
K3\times\mathbb{B}_{2}
, which in turn is
dual the heterotic string on
CY3_{\text{{het}}}\times\mathbb{T}_{M}^{3}
with
\mathbb{T}_{M}^{3}=\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{S}_{M}^{1}

and elliptically fibered Calabi-Yau threefold
CY3_{\text{{het}}}.

As such M-theory on
K3\times K3\times\mathbb{S}_{M}^{1}
, is dual to
heterotic string on
CY3_{\text{{het}}}\times\mathbb{T}_{M}^{3}

with
CY3_{\text{{het}}}
having both an elliptic and K3 fibrations as

CY3_{\text{{het}}}=\mathbb{T}^{2}\times K3,\qquad\mathbb{T}_{M}^{3}=\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{S}_{M}^{1}

(253)

Moreover, non-BPS particles states are given by wrapping M5 wrapping
K3\times\mathbb{S}_{M}^{1};
the wrapping of M5 on
K3
gives the so-called
Maldacena-Strominger-Witten (MSW) string. The wrapping of this solitonic MSW
string on
\mathbb{S}_{M}^{1}
gives non-BPS particle states, which in the
dual string theory, is described by left moving excitations
n_{L}
of a
heterotic string as in (
252
).
